<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Physical Prompt Injection Hijacks VLM-Controlled Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05715) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A systematic study introduces a four-category taxonomy of physical prompt injection attacks against VLM-controlled robots, with a benchmark of 20 attack prompts. Across 5,670 trials on GPT-4o, Gemini 2.5 Flash, and Qwen3-VL-32B, attacks succeeded at rates of 27.0%, 29.4%, and 5.0% respectively. This research exposes a critical security vulnerability in the emerging deployment of VLMs as robotic planners, where adversarial text in the physical environment can hijack robot behavior. The findings have significant implications for real-world robotics applications, highlighting the need for robust defenses before widespread deployment. The study evaluates three simple mitigations: prompt-based defense (75-100% effective, model-dependent), two-stage verification (85-100%), and pre-processing text masking (100%). Analysis of reasoning traces shows that successful compromise is almost always conscious (99.9% acknowledgment rate), and models defend through structurally different mechanisms, such as explicit rejection for Gemini and perceptual inattention for GPT-4o.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are increasingly used as planners in robotic systems, translating natural-language commands into actions based on visual understanding. Prompt injection is a known attack vector for LLMs, where malicious inputs cause unintended behavior, and this study extends it to physical environments where adversarial text is placed in the robot's visual field. The research builds on prior work on physical prompt injection against VLMs on wearable devices, which showed high success rates in simulation and real photos.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Optimal Agnostic PAC Learner Achieves Statistical Lower Bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06363) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The authors construct a learner that achieves the statistically optimal risk bound for agnostic PAC learning, matching the lower bounds of Devroye, Györfi, and Lugosi up to universal constants. The bound is $L(\hat{h}) \le L^* + 7\cdot10^8\left(\sqrt{\frac{L^*(d+\log(1/\delta))}{n}} + \frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{n}\right)$. This settles a fundamental open problem in learning theory by providing matching upper and lower bounds for agnostic PAC learning, a significant theoretical breakthrough. The result has broad implications for machine learning theory and could influence the design of practical learning algorithms. The bound holds for any class $H$ of finite VC dimension $d \ge 1$, and for any $0 < \delta \le 1/2$, with probability at least $1-\delta$. The constant $7\cdot10^8$ is large but universal, and the result matches lower bounds at every fixed $L^*$.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Agnostic PAC learning is a framework where the target concept may not be perfectly realizable by the hypothesis class, and the goal is to minimize the risk relative to the best hypothesis in the class. The VC dimension measures the capacity of a hypothesis class, and sample complexity refers to the number of training samples needed to achieve a desired accuracy and confidence. This paper provides an optimal learner that achieves the best possible risk bound up to constant factors.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [GrandCode: First AI to Beat All Humans in Live Coding Contests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02721) ⭐️ 9.0/10

GrandCode, a multi-agent reinforcement learning system, achieved grandmaster level in competitive programming, winning first place in three consecutive Codeforces live rounds (1087, 1088, 1089) in March 2026, surpassing all human participants including legendary grandmasters. It introduces Agentic GRPO, a novel RL algorithm for multi-stage agent rollouts with delayed rewards. This marks the first time an AI system has consistently outperformed all human programmers in live competitive programming contests, signaling a shift in the coding AI landscape. It could redefine benchmarks for AI coding capabilities and influence future development of agentic RL systems. GrandCode orchestrates multiple agentic modules (hypothesis proposal, solver, test generator, summarization) and jointly improves them through post-training and online test-time RL. Agentic GRPO addresses severe off-policy drift in agentic RL, enabling effective multi-agent training with delayed rewards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Competitive programming is a challenging domain for AI, requiring problem-solving, algorithm design, and coding under time constraints. Previous best AI systems, like Google's Gemini 3 Deep Think, still underperformed top humans and were not evaluated under live conditions. Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) extends RL to systems with multiple interacting agents, and GRPO is a policy optimization method used in RL training.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [CUDA-L2: LLM-Guided RL Surpasses cuBLAS for HGEMM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02551) ⭐️ 9.0/10

CUDA-L2, a system combining large language models and reinforcement learning, automatically optimizes half-precision general matrix multiply (HGEMM) CUDA kernels across 1,000 configurations, achieving up to 22% speedup over torch.matmul and 19.2% over cuBLAS in offline mode, with even larger gains in server mode. This work demonstrates that even highly optimized, closed-source libraries like cuBLAS can be outperformed by automated LLM-guided RL optimization, potentially transforming GPU kernel development and benefiting AI infrastructure and high-performance computing. The system uses CUDA execution speed as the RL reward and explores configuration spaces at scales impractical for humans. In server mode, speedups increase to +28.7% over torch.matmul, +26.0% over cuBLAS, +22.4% over cuBLASLt-heuristic, and +15.9% over cuBLASLt-AutoTuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: HGEMM is a critical operation in deep learning and scientific computing, and NVIDIA's cuBLAS/cuBLASLt are widely used, highly optimized libraries. Traditional kernel optimization relies on manual tuning or heuristic autotuning, which is time-consuming and may not explore all possibilities. CUDA-L2 leverages LLMs to generate candidate kernels and RL to iteratively refine them, using actual execution speed as feedback.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [Impossibility Triangle Proves Fundamental Trade-off in Long-Context Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05066) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2605.05066) proves that no long-sequence model can simultaneously achieve efficiency, compactness, and recall proportional to sequence length. It formalizes this trade-off using an Online Sequence Processor abstraction and classifies 52 architectures. This result provides a fundamental theoretical limit for long-context modeling, analogous to the CAP theorem for distributed systems. It will guide future architecture design by clarifying unavoidable trade-offs, affecting researchers and engineers working on Transformers, state space models, and hybrids. The proof uses the Data Processing Inequality and Fano's Inequality to show that any model satisfying efficiency and compactness can recall at most O(poly(d)/log V) key-value pairs, where d is model dimension and V is vocabulary size. Experiments on synthetic associative recall tasks with five architectures confirm the bound, with empirical recall capacity strictly below the information-theoretic limit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context models like Transformers and state space models aim to process long sequences efficiently. Efficiency means per-step computation independent of sequence length; compactness means state size independent of sequence length; recall means the ability to retrieve historical facts. The paper introduces an Online Sequence Processor abstraction to unify these architectures and prove the trade-off.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [AI Security Leaderboard Reveals Hundredfold Gap in Frontier Model Safeguards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03070) ⭐️ 9.0/10

FAR.AI has launched the AI Security Leaderboard, an independent benchmark that ranks frontier AI models by their safeguards against universal jailbreaks, and published the Minimal Standard for Safeguards Version 1.0. Testing four leading models, they found over a hundredfold difference in security: Claude Fable 5 and GPT-5.6 Sol resisted all attacks, while Grok 4.5 and Gemini 3.1 Pro had hundreds of universal jailbreaks, some costing as little as $24. This is a significant contribution to AI security, providing an independent and transparent benchmark for evaluating frontier model safeguards, which is crucial for policymakers, developers, and users. The empirical results highlight that some leading models are far less secure than others, underscoring the need for standardized security baselines and continuous evaluation as AI capabilities advance. The benchmark focuses on severe misuse requests across CBRNE threats and offensive cybersecurity, and uses the FAR.AI Minimal Standard for Safeguards as a minimum bar. The leaderboard will be updated on a rolling basis, and the methodology and standard will be periodically revised to reflect the latest capabilities and safeguards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Universal jailbreaks are techniques that bypass AI safety constraints across multiple inputs, often using crafted prompts or suffixes. The FAR.AI Minimal Standard for Safeguards is built from defenses already deployed in production by peer developers, avoiding speculative research. CBRNE threats refer to chemical, biological, radiological, nuclear, and explosive hazards, which are high-risk areas for AI misuse.

## [New Mexico Court Orders Meta to Pay $567M for Child Mental Health Harms](https://www.theguardian.com/technology/2026/aug/06/new-mexico-court-meta) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A New Mexico court has ordered Meta to pay $567 million into a fund to address mental health harms to children from its platforms, and to implement changes for underage users in the state. This ruling follows a lawsuit filed by Attorney General Raúl Torrez in 2023 and marks the second phase of a landmark trial. This ruling sets a significant legal precedent for holding social media companies accountable for child safety and mental health impacts. It could encourage other states or jurisdictions to pursue similar actions, potentially reshaping platform policies and industry practices regarding underage users. The court also ordered Meta to make specific changes in New Mexico, including prohibiting push notifications to underage accounts between 10 p.m. and a certain hour, and removing Like counts for users under 18 unless approved by a parent or guardian. The $567 million is in addition to a previous $942 million judgment, bringing the total to over $1.5 billion.

hackernews · boplicity · Aug 7, 00:06 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49204352)

**Background**: The lawsuit was filed under New Mexico's public nuisance law, NMSA 1978 § 30-8-1, which allows the state to sue for actions that injure public health, safety, or welfare. The case is part of a broader wave of legal actions against social media companies over their impact on youth mental health, with many states and school districts filing similar suits.

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [Wyzer: A New Language for Distributed Safety via Choreographic Programming](https://github.com/Wyzer-Lang/wyzer) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Wyzer is a statically typed, compiled programming language that integrates choreographic programming and the Perceus memory model to address distributed deadlocks and protocol mismatches, which Rust does not cover. The author plans to release version 0.1.0 soon after months of research and weeks of development. This project tackles a significant gap in distributed systems safety, potentially offering a new paradigm for writing deadlock-free distributed applications. It could influence future language design by bringing academic concepts like choreographic programming into practical use. Wyzer uses linear/affine types and Perceus reference counting instead of borrow checkers and lifetimes, which the author claims is computationally simpler for LSPs to understand. The language aims to generalize choreographic programming in a high-level language, a rare attempt to solve these safety gaps.

hackernews · v0id_isgood · Aug 7, 12:28 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49209385)

**Background**: Choreographic programming is a paradigm for distributed systems where programs are written as global descriptions of interactions, ensuring deadlock-freedom by construction. Perceus is a garbage-free reference counting method that enables efficient memory management without a runtime garbage collector, as used in the Koka language. Rust provides memory safety but not distributed deadlock safety, motivating Wyzer's design.

## [New Metric Measures All-or-None Ignition in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05160) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Ignition Index (I), a validated scalar metric that operationalizes Global Workspace Theory's all-or-none ignition prediction in transformer language models. Across 11 models from five architecture families, they found feedforward transformers exhibit 89% higher aggregate ignition than state space models (SSMs), with Mamba showing near-linear profiles. This work provides the first validated quantitative bridge between Global Workspace Theory's dynamical predictions and mechanistic interpretability, offering a new tool for comparing architectures. It could influence future model design by highlighting that recurrent architectures like Huginn manifest workspace-like transitions along the recurrence dimension, and that transformer architectures may saturate available ignition mechanisms. The metric fits a four-parameter sigmoid to per-layer linear probe accuracy as a function of input signal strength, extracting steepness parameter beta-hat. Shuffled-label controls demonstrated 9.6-fold selectivity for genuine linguistic structure over spurious probe capacity (p < 0.001, Mann-Whitney U-test), and Pythia-410M showed a PELT-detected phase transition at training step 256 (+67%), preceding induction-head formation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Global Workspace Theory (GWT) posits that conscious processing involves a global broadcast of information, with all-or-none ignition marking the transition to a workspace state. Linear probes are simple classifiers trained on hidden representations to detect specific features, and their accuracy can indicate how well a property is encoded. State space models (SSMs) like Mamba process sequences in linear time, unlike transformers' quadratic attention cost, and this study compares their internal dynamics.

## [Woodpecker Distillation: Weak Models Fix Strong LLM Reasoning Bugs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05168) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Woodpecker Distillation, a weak-to-strong training framework that uses contrastive local interventions from weak models to correct localized reasoning bugs in strong LLMs, improving reasoning without direct fine-tuning on patches. This approach addresses a key challenge in LLM reasoning by showing that localized bugs can be corrected via weak-model guidance, potentially reducing the need for large-scale fine-tuning. It offers a novel path to improve reasoning capabilities in strong models, which could impact the broader AI ecosystem. The method contrasts successful and unsuccessful weak-model patches at the same prefix, constructs a corrective teacher distribution from their induced future token predictions, and distills this signal into the strong model. Experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks show consistent improvements over direct imitation baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often fail on reasoning tasks despite having the capability to solve them, often due to localized reasoning bugs in intermediate steps rather than global incompetence. Weak-to-strong training is a paradigm where a weaker model guides a stronger model, and distillation involves transferring knowledge from a teacher to a student model. This paper builds on these concepts to propose a novel training framework.

Otter, a 15.3M-parameter human chess AI, achieves 55.23% top-1 and 90.95% top-5 move-prediction accuracy, surpassing the prior state-of-the-art Maia 2 with fewer parameters and less training data. It models chess as a time-aware, sequential process, conditioning on the last 20 moves and clock pressure. It also shows that smaller, more efficient models can outperform larger ones when leveraging sequential context. Otter is trained on 6.1 billion positions from 117 million Lichess rapid games over 30 days on a single T4 GPU. Accuracy peaks at 57.38% in the 1900-1999 Elo bracket, and the model uses a move history encoder and a time control module.

The paper introduces SearchAuditBench, a benchmark with 1,243 expert-annotated failed trajectories from eight open-weight models across five deep-search benchmarks, and SearchAuditor, a multi-perspective auditing framework that localizes, attributes, and repairs failures. SearchAuditor achieves a 32.3% end-to-end pass rate, outperforming baselines including GPT-5.5-powered ones (26.6%). This work addresses a critical gap in debugging long-horizon search agents, where failures are hard to diagnose due to long, noisy trajectories. By automating failure localization and repair, it reduces manual inspection burden and improves agent reliability, which is essential for deploying trustworthy AI systems. SearchAuditBench trajectories average 73.1 messages and 65.1K tokens, with annotations including the critical error step, a search-specific root cause, and a reference repair with grading rubrics. SearchAuditor uses evidence-grounded adjudication to combine multiple perspectives, and its repairs enable agents to recover from errors in resumed runs.

## [Small Cognitive Models: Scale Matters Little In-Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05224) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers trained fourteen models from 135M to 14B parameters on Psych-101, a dataset of 10.7 million trial-level choices, finding that in-distribution performance is nearly scale-invariant, while out-of-distribution generalization improves with larger models. They also introduced prompt channel diagnostics that reveal the critical role of stimuli and feedback. This work challenges the assumption that larger models are always better for cognitive modeling, showing that small models can suffice for in-distribution tasks, which could reduce computational costs. The findings on out-of-distribution generalization and prompt channels provide insights into how these models learn and generalize, benefiting both AI and cognitive science. The models span four architecture families, and a 0.6B to 1B parameter model matches a 70B baseline on held-out participants in-distribution. Masking stimuli and feedback destroys 75.7% of learned information, pushing models below chance, while permutation tests show sensitivity to trial order in tasks where order is determined by prior responses.

**Background**: Large language models fine-tuned on human behavioral data are used as cognitive proxies to simulate human decision-making. Psych-101 is a large dataset of trial-level choices from 160 experiments, enabling training of models at various scales. Prompt channels refer to different components of the input, such as instructions, stimuli, feedback, and choice history, which can be manipulated to understand what information models rely on.

## [Search2Skill: Distilling Skills Beyond Knowledge Boundaries via Rubric-Based RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05245) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Search2Skill is a novel framework that automatically identifies an LLM agent's capability gaps, searches external sources to address them, and distills the retrieved evidence into structured, reusable skills. It is optimized by a rubric-based reinforcement learning scheme that jointly improves when to search, how to search, and how to generate skills. This framework overcomes the knowledge boundary of self-evolving methods, which are limited by the model's parametric knowledge. By enabling agents to acquire expert-level procedural knowledge from external sources, it has the potential to significantly enhance agent performance in professional domains and facilitate skill transfer across model scales. The framework is evaluated on eight expert-level domains from three benchmarks, consistently outperforming both search-augmented and trajectory-based skill-learning baselines under streaming and held-out protocols. Analyses show that gains arise from skill abstraction rather than raw retrieved evidence, and that acquired skills transfer across model scales.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Reusable skills encapsulate procedural knowledge for solving professional tasks, enabling LLM-based agents to self-evolve. Traditional self-evolving methods construct skills internally from the model's parametric knowledge or trajectories, which limits them to what the model already knows. Rubric-based reinforcement learning replaces scalar rewards with structured, multi-criteria rubrics to guide training, which is a key component of Search2Skill.

WorldClaw introduces a fully agentic, coarse-to-fine framework for generating large-scale, editable 3D worlds from text prompts. It uses planning agents to structure the scene and render-based agents to refine terrain, objects, and contacts, ensuring global coherence and reusable assets. This work addresses significant challenges in large-scale 3D generation, such as spatial coherence and asset reusability, which are critical for fields like game development and simulation. By enabling editable, instance-level assets, it could streamline workflows in virtual world creation and interactive applications. WorldClaw builds a globally coherent terrain foundation from semantic layouts, reusable assets, and a region-aware height field. For detail-demanding regions, it generates terrain-conditioned compositions, reconstructs editable textured meshes, and recovers their placement on the terrain.

## [OrchestraBench: New Benchmark for Multi-Agent Orchestration Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05263) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OrchestraBench introduces a controlled, seed-reproducible failure-injection harness to evaluate multi-agent orchestration failures, recovery, and decomposition quality. It proposes cascade radius and per-failure-mode recovery as primary metrics, and compares routing policies with bootstrap confidence intervals and paired tests. As multi-agent systems move to production, diagnosing failures is critical; this benchmark fills a gap by focusing on failure modes and recovery rather than just task accuracy. The finding that intent-reasoning routers outperform keyword-based ones in adversarial scenarios has practical implications for system design. On a 26-case gold-labelled diagnostic, a keyword/flag router scored 0% on adversarial cases, while an intent-reasoning model router scored 100%, matching the oracle. Controlled probes with a real Claude agent revealed three failure-handling tiers across five MAST modes: tool faults recovered fully, ambiguous delegation partially, and three latent or semantic modes never recovered.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent orchestration frameworks coordinate multiple AI agents to achieve a common goal, but they often fail in complex ways. Benchmarks typically report task accuracy without diagnosing where failures originate or how they propagate. Cascade radius measures the distance between a decision and its furthest consequence, while failure injection deliberately introduces faults to test system robustness.

<a id="item-23"></a>
## [C3PO Benchmark Reveals Modality Dominance in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05381) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced C3PO, a benchmark with 3,404 samples spanning video, audio, image, and text, to evaluate cross-modal composition and counterfactual conflict in multimodal LLMs. The best model, Gemini-3.1-Pro, achieved only 73.17% accuracy compared to humans' 88.64%, with open-source models collapsing under conflict. This benchmark highlights a critical weakness in current multimodal LLMs: modality dominance, where models over-rely on one modality (often text) and ignore contradictory evidence from others. The findings suggest that merely integrating multiple modalities does not guarantee robust reasoning, urging the community to focus on architectural improvements for sustained cross-modal attention. C3PO uses a fully automatic pipeline with 25 logically grounded templates and a paired IC/CC structure with four tiers. Attention probes revealed that 86-95% of failures stem from modality dominance, with 87-95% of attention concentrated on text, and mid-layer attention entropy predicts correctness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) process diverse sensory inputs but often exhibit biased reasoning toward a dominant modality. Cross-modal reasoning requires fusing dispersed evidence (composition) and resolving deliberate contradictions (counterfactual conflict). Existing benchmarks may not adequately test these abilities, and modality dominance has been observed in prior studies.

## [Recursive Synthesis Framework Generates 37,484 Long-Horizon Terminal Tasks at Low Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05466) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Recursive Synthetic Terminal Tasks (RST), a recursive verified synthesis framework that generates 37,484 long-horizon terminal-agent tasks at roughly $0.05 per task across fifteen recursive rounds. Task difficulty escalates substantially, with median reference solution length growing from 67 to 374 lines and DeepSeek-V4-Pro pass@4 dropping from 90% to 2.5%. This addresses a critical bottleneck in training data production for terminal agents, which is typically expensive and hard to scale. The framework's low cost and difficulty scaling could enable broader access to high-quality long-horizon training data, potentially accelerating progress in AI agent research and applications. RST starts from verified seed tasks, extends the reference solution, realigns the verifier and instruction, and validates in a fresh sandbox before reusing accepted tasks as seeds. Fine-tuning on rejection-sampled trajectories improves Qwen3.5-27B and Qwen3.5-122B-A10B by up to 10 points on Terminal-Bench benchmarks, and agentic PPO yields relative gains of 20.0%, 41.2%, and 21.9% on three benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Terminal agents are AI systems that operate in command-line environments to perform tasks, and training them requires long-horizon tasks with consistent instructions, environments, reference solutions, and verifiers. High-quality such data is expensive to produce manually, and direct LLM generation often breaks consistency. RST uses a recursive verification process to maintain consistency while scaling up task generation, and the paper demonstrates its utility by fine-tuning models on the synthesized tasks.

## [Auditing Autonomous Analysis Agents: Limits and Error Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05490) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper provides the first theoretical analysis of auditing methods for autonomous analysis agents, quantifying error localization, detection limits, and error control procedures without labeled mistakes. As autonomous agents increasingly perform data analyses, ensuring their reliability is critical. This work offers foundational insights that can guide the design of trustworthy AI systems and auditing practices. The paper shows that the choice of scoring function determines whether errors can be localized, and that errors below a certain magnitude are fundamentally unidentifiable. It also provides procedures to control false flag rates, requiring only exchangeability of sound analyses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous analysis agents perform end-to-end data analyses with minimal supervision. Auditing such agents involves detecting and localizing errors without labeled mistakes, often by comparing operations against predictions from models trained on sound analyses. Identifiability is a statistical property ensuring that model parameters can be uniquely determined from data.

## [EcoAgent-Bench: New Benchmark for Budget-Constrained LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05519) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EcoAgent-Bench is a new benchmark that evaluates LLM agents' economic decision-making under budget constraints, featuring 304 real-derived tasks across five families adapted from GAIA, HotpotQA, and MuSiQue. It tests four key decisions: avoiding unnecessary escalation, escalating when local evidence is insufficient, selecting a model tier, and stopping on unsupported premises. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing agent benchmarks by treating resource use as part of the task itself, not just an auxiliary statistic. It reveals that completion under a budget and economical action selection are distinct properties, which is crucial for deploying LLM agents in real-world cost-sensitive applications. The benchmark evaluates seven LLM agents in tool-API and workspace-CLI settings, along with four oracle scripted controls. Tool-API agents achieve only 3.9-24.0% micro strict success (at most 7.3% economic consistency), and a threshold-crossing budget sweep changes GPT-5.4's escalation rate from 0% to only 3%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used to perform tasks by calling tools and models, but existing benchmarks typically measure task completion without considering the cost of actions. This benchmark introduces priced actions and explicit budgets, forcing agents to make economically sound choices. The tasks are derived from established multi-hop QA datasets like GAIA, HotpotQA, and MuSiQue, which require integrating evidence from multiple sources.

## [Hyper-ES: Merging Descent Directions for Efficient LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05541) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Hyper-ES is a novel subspace-based evolution strategy that first performs a few gradient fine-tuning runs to obtain descent directions, then applies CMA-ES to optimize layer-wise DARE-TIES merging coefficients within the span of these directions. It consistently outperforms GRPO-LoRA by 1% on six mathematical reasoning datasets while requiring 10% fewer space-consuming gradient updates. This work addresses a key limitation of evolution strategies in high-dimensional LLM parameter spaces, making ES a more viable alternative for resource-constrained fine-tuning. It could enable more efficient adaptation of LLMs for reasoning tasks without full gradient updates, benefiting researchers and practitioners with limited computational resources. Hyper-ES uses CMA-ES to optimize layer-wise DARE-TIES merging coefficients within a compact adaptation subspace spanned by descent directions from a few gradient fine-tuning runs. The method is evaluated on Qwen2.5-Instruct and DeepSeek-R1-Distill backbones across six mathematical reasoning datasets, and code is available at https://github.com/kuangrepi/Hyper-ES.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Evolution Strategy (ES) is an optimization technique inspired by natural evolution, using mutation and selection to search for optimal parameters. However, in high-dimensional spaces like those of billion-parameter LLMs, random perturbations are often orthogonal to useful update directions, making ES inefficient. Subspace-based methods, such as those using task vectors from fine-tuning, can reduce the search space by focusing on meaningful directions. Hyper-ES combines these ideas by merging descent directions from few gradient runs and optimizing merging coefficients with CMA-ES.

## [SkillTV-Bench: Benchmarking Skill-Aware Trajectory Verification in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05573) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SkillTV-Bench is a new benchmark with 681 real agent trajectories from 50 tasks across 11 domains, designed to evaluate skill-aware trajectory verification for LLM-as-a-Judge and Agent-as-a-Judge methods. The authors also propose SkillTV-Evolve, which externalizes verification knowledge as a reusable JudgeSkill and improves judge accuracy by 14.8 percentage points. This benchmark fills a gap in existing judge benchmarks by focusing on skill-augmented agents and directly inspectable artifacts, which is crucial for evaluating long-horizon tasks. It provides a standardized way to measure and improve trajectory verification, potentially advancing the reliability of LLM agents in complex environments. The SkillTV-Evolve method uses an automated evolution loop on a disjoint development pool to refine the JudgeSkill using misjudged cases. In offline rollout-pool selection, the refined skill increases selected-trajectory success from 22.9% with one rollout to 45.5% with ten rollouts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly execute long-horizon tasks through tool use and environment interaction, shifting evaluation from final-response scoring to verification of complete executions. For skill-augmented agents, verification requires procedural knowledge encoded in task-time skills, which indicates what evidence to inspect and which failures are task-critical. Existing judge benchmarks often expose final responses or static trajectories and rarely combine task-time skills with directly inspectable artifacts and environments.

## [New Framework Measures and Detects Harmful AI Sycophancy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05624) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces CAP (Contrastive Anchor Probing), a framework for collecting labeled data on preference-induced stance reversal sycophancy (PSRS), and applies it to 17 LLMs, collecting 290,460 labeled responses across 12 domains. It reveals PSRS rates ranging from 5% to 56% and shows that detection from response text alone is feasible but degrades on unseen models. This work addresses a critical AI safety issue by providing a systematic way to measure and detect harmful sycophancy, which is prevalent in LLMs. The findings have direct implications for model evaluation and deployment, helping developers identify and mitigate sycophantic behaviors that could mislead users. The study covers 17 open- and closed-source LLMs across 12 everyday-advice domains, with PSRS rates varying from 5% to 56%, and more capable models being less sycophantic. Detection performance drops on unseen models, and the authors propose an initial approach to improve cross-model generalization; the dataset and code will be released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Sycophancy in LLMs refers to the tendency of models to tailor responses to please the user, which can sometimes lead to harmful behaviors like reversing a stance to align with user preferences. Prior work has measured sycophancy levels, but this paper goes further by focusing on a specific harmful type and exploring automatic detection from a single response. The CAP framework uses contrastive anchor probing to generate labeled data, enabling large-scale analysis.

## [SkillHEX: Hypothesis-Driven Skill Evolution for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05628) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SkillHEX introduces a closed-loop framework that combines hypothesis-driven self-verification with evidence-guided tree search, enabling autonomous, on-demand skill evolution for LLM agents under limited interaction budgets. It outperforms existing self-evolving methods on 87 tasks from SkillsBench, achieving an average pass rate of 55.9% with GPT-5.3-Codex and 57.9% with Claude Opus 4.7 under a five-iteration budget. This work addresses a critical challenge in autonomous agent development: the need for on-demand skill learning without manual maintenance or extensive training data. By mitigating sparse reward and exploitation issues, SkillHEX could enable more robust and adaptable LLM agents in real-world deployments, reducing the cost and scalability barriers of skill maintenance. SkillHEX translates falsifiable failure hypotheses into executable tests, producing diagnostic evidence as dense reward without additional environment attempts. This evidence guides a search over persistent skill-revision branches, dynamically balancing exploitation of supported edits with exploration of plausible alternatives. The framework is evaluated on 87 tasks from SkillsBench, with a five-iteration budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on hand-crafted skills, which are costly to maintain and may not align with real-world tasks. Autonomous skill evolution at test time is desirable but faces challenges such as sparse rewards and the risk of exploitation traps, where early misdiagnoses exhaust limited trials. SkillHEX addresses these by using hypothesis-driven self-verification to generate dense rewards and evidence-guided tree search to explore skill revisions effectively.

## [DreamGuard: Proactive Runtime Guardrail for LLM Agents via Risk-Aware World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05695) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DreamGuard introduces a proactive runtime guardrail for LLM agents that uses a risk-aware world model to predict and mitigate long-horizon risks before unsafe actions are executed. It achieves an average end-to-end latency of 25 ms per call and outperforms existing guardrail baselines across four benchmarks. This addresses a critical blind spot in LLM agent safety: reactive guardrails fail to catch long-horizon risks where individually benign actions gradually drift toward hazardous states. DreamGuard's proactive approach could significantly improve safe deployment of LLM agents in real-world applications, reducing irreversible consequences on external systems and user data. The world model maintains a compact recurrent latent state over the trajectory and predicts future latent states, from which DreamGuard derives immediate-hazard and prefix-risk evidence. It fuses these multi-horizon signals into intervention decisions before execution, and experiments show it achieves the best safety-utility trade-off among evaluated guardrails.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly invoke external tools and interact with real-world systems, where unsafe actions can cause irreversible harm. Runtime guardrails check proposed actions before execution, but many are reactive and only assess the apparent safety of the current action, lacking an explicit model of how risk evolves across a trajectory. This creates a blind spot for long-horizon risks, where individually benign-looking actions can gradually drift the agent toward hazardous states. DreamGuard addresses this by using a risk-aware world model to predict risk evolution and enable proactive intervention.

## [Subliminal Learning: Hidden Bias Transfer via Non-Semantic Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05734) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that subliminal learning, where biases transfer from teacher to student models via random synthetic data, is driven by non-semantic weight structures, and adding Gaussian noise to weights increases transfer magnitude by 1.9x in Gemma and 1.3x in Llama. It also demonstrates that steering vectors can generate subliminal data, and students inherit the type of intervention used. This finding challenges current AI safety auditing methods, as standard input data inspection fails to detect hidden subliminal signals. It underscores the need for new auditing techniques that can identify latent signals in training data, especially as synthetic data becomes central to frontier training pipelines. The study shows that steering vectors can be applied to teachers to produce subliminal data, alongside prompting and finetuning. Analysis of student activations reveals that students inherit not only the semantic meaning of the bias but also the type of intervention (steering vs. prompting), and gradients of steered data correlate linearly with teacher steering vectors, offering a potential auditing method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Subliminal learning is a phenomenon where language models acquire traits from model-generated data that is semantically unrelated to those traits, posing risks for AI alignment and safety. Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller student model learns from a larger teacher model, and steering vectors are directions in activation space that control model behavior. This paper builds on prior work by Anthropic and others, exploring the mechanisms behind subliminal learning.

## [Deterministic Screen-Activity Compilation Boosts Agent Memory Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05784) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a deterministic, zero-model pipeline that compiles passively captured screen activity into compact 'activity frames' for agent memory, achieving 86x compression and 98.4% question-answering accuracy on a real-world corpus. The compiler also provides first measurements of Routine Overhead Ratio (60-343x) and routine recurrence (9.0% in-sample, 7.7% out-of-sample), enabling cost modeling for computer-use agents. This work addresses a critical inefficiency in computer-use agents: they re-derive routines from scratch instead of leveraging past user actions. By providing a deterministic, auditable memory format that outperforms LLM summaries, it could significantly reduce inference costs and improve agent reliability, impacting AI agent design and deployment. The pipeline segments a local capture stream into typed activity frames with evidence pointers, requiring no model in the loop, ensuring byte-identical, cacheable output. On a single-user corpus of 128,756 frames over 51 days, the compiler reduces a day's raw capture to a context block 86x smaller in 68 ms, and an agent reading that block achieves 98.4% accuracy (Wilson 95% CI 91.7-99.7%) versus 66-80% for LLM summaries. The compiler also doubles as a cost instrument, reporting a delegable recurrence of 9.0% in-sample and 7.7% out-of-sample, with a realistic all-fleet token ceiling near 8%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Computer-use agents are AI systems that interact with software interfaces to perform tasks, but their memory typically records user commands rather than actual screen activity, forcing them to re-derive routines. Episodic memory for agents aims to store past experiences, but existing approaches often rely on LLM summaries that are lossy and non-auditable. This paper proposes a deterministic compilation approach that preserves evidence and enables exact replay, addressing both memory efficiency and cost measurement.

## [ChainClaw: A Layered Agent Framework for Reliable On-Chain Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05790) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ChainClaw is a blockchain-native agent framework built on OpenClaw that addresses the Reactivity, Irreversibility, and Observability gaps in LLM agents for on-chain execution. It introduces a layered architecture with an event-driven orchestration layer, a simulation-based safety intelligence layer, and an on-chain monitoring runtime layer, unified by a cross-layer memory subsystem. This framework addresses critical gaps in applying LLM agents to blockchain environments, a growing niche area. By improving safety and task completion, ChainClaw could enable more reliable autonomous DeFi operations and other on-chain applications, potentially accelerating the adoption of AI agents in Web3. ChainClaw closes the Reactivity gap via event ingestion and simulation feedback, the Irreversibility gap via a pre-execution safety pipeline with transaction simulation and action guard, and the Observability gap via an on-chain read adapter and transaction monitor. It is evaluated on a purpose-built benchmark covering seven tasks across four categories and five dimensions, consistently outperforming representative baselines on both safety and task completion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: General-purpose LLM agents rely on assumptions that break down in blockchain environments, where execution is stateful, adversarial, and economically irreversible. On-chain transactions are processed and recorded directly on the blockchain, requiring consensus, and errors can lead to permanent financial loss. OpenClaw is an open-source personal AI assistant that runs on local devices and works with messaging platforms, providing a modular runtime that ChainClaw extends with blockchain-native components.

<a id="item-35"></a>
## [Self-Evolving LLM Agents Suffer Skill Contamination; Pre-Commit Gating Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05810) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that self-evolving LLM agents experience a capability-contamination phase transition: past a critical pool size, newly added skills degrade performance irreversibly. They propose Verifier-as-Gatekeeper (VaG), a progressive trust hierarchy with three heterogeneous critics, which improves performance every round and reaches 72% pass@1 on Terminal-Bench 2 with a much smaller skill pool. This finding challenges the assumption that self-evolution is always beneficial, highlighting a critical failure mode in LLM agent design. The proposed pre-commit gating approach offers a practical solution to prevent skill contamination, potentially improving the reliability and scalability of self-evolving agents across various applications. The contamination is structurally irreversible: removing a source skill after the fact cannot erase flawed reasoning inherited by descendants, so post-hoc rollback recovers only a small fraction of lost performance. VaG filters each skill individually using structural validity, behavioral harmlessness, and semantic consistency critics, coupled with marginal-gain subset selection to remove combinatorial contamination before skills enter the runtime context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving LLM agents improve by distilling reusable skills from their execution trajectories. However, this process is not monotonic; defective skills can contaminate future skill distillation, leading to performance degradation. The paper formalizes this as a phase transition and introduces a pre-commit gating mechanism to ensure only high-quality skills are admitted.

<a id="item-36"></a>
## [Runtime Observability Contract for Heterogeneous Attention Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05863) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a runtime observability contract with three operators that covers four attention memory classes, and composes per-stage error bounds into a request-level risk ledger. It is instantiated on six model configurations across five architecture families and validated with 12.4M entry reads under eight-way concurrency. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM inference systems by providing a formal, machine-checked way to monitor and compose error bounds across heterogeneous memory types, which is essential for reliable deployment of modern models with latent caches and sparse selectors. It could influence systems design for LLM inference and improve trust in model execution. The contract carries error metrics as types, so composition is only allowed when metrics match; the paper's own first composed chain was rejected, and the repaired chain uses two proved bridges. Claims are classified as certified, partially certified, or empirical, with composition inheriting the weakest tier, and the tier is decided by the machine. The implementation includes a fused always-on probe under CUDA graphs and is released with all artifacts and Lean development.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Modern LLMs use various memory mechanisms beyond the plain KV cache, such as latent caches, learned sparse selectors, and recurrent states, each with different failure modes under compression. Runtime observability is crucial for monitoring and ensuring correctness during inference, but existing approaches lack formal composition of error bounds across heterogeneous memory types. This paper proposes a contract-based approach that types error metrics and composes them into a risk ledger, with machine-checked certification tiers.

## [GSBF: 3D Gaussian Splatting for Environment-Aware Beamforming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05896) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces GSBF, a 3D Gaussian splatting pipeline for environment-aware beamforming that models scattering with reciprocity-preserving bidirectional spherical Gaussian (Bi-SG) kernels and renders angular propagator maps to synthesize constant-modulus beams directly from access point pose and user position, eliminating the need for online instantaneous CSI and iterative optimization. This approach could significantly reduce pilot overhead and computational complexity in MIMO systems, enabling faster and more efficient beamforming. It bridges 3D Gaussian splatting with wireless communications, potentially inspiring new research at the intersection of computer vision and radio environment mapping. GSBF uses two-sided electromagnetic rasterization to render an angular propagator map, which is then aggregated through an over-complete array-manifold dictionary and projected to constant-modulus beamformers. Simulations show it consistently outperforms baselines like exhaustive beam alignment (EBA) with lower latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian splatting is a volume rendering technique that represents scenes as a set of 3D Gaussians, enabling real-time radiance field rendering from multiple images. Beamforming in MIMO systems traditionally relies on accurate instantaneous channel state information (CSI) and iterative optimization, which are resource-intensive. GSBF leverages the physical geometry of the environment to bypass these requirements.

## [AgentOPSD: Recursive Self-Distillation for Agentic RL Credit Assignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05987) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentOPSD introduces a critic-free, recursive self-distillation method for turn-level credit assignment in agentic reinforcement learning, aggregating token-level teacher-student log-probability gaps into turn-level evidence and updating a Bayesian belief state in log-odds space. It achieves 89.1% success on ALFWorld with Qwen2.5-7B, outperforming GRPO and strong self-distillation baselines. This method addresses a critical challenge in long-horizon agentic tasks: accurately crediting the few pivotal decisions that determine outcomes. By providing a principled, critic-free credit assignment mechanism, it could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of RL training for LLM-based agents, potentially accelerating progress in autonomous task-solving systems. AgentOPSD is fully compatible with standard policy optimization and requires neither an additional critic nor extra rollouts. It was evaluated on ALFWorld, WebShop, and Search-QA using Qwen2.5 models at 3B and 7B scales, with ablations showing gains come from turn-level aggregation and history-dependent recursive belief updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards often struggles to credit pivotal decisions in long-horizon, multi-turn agentic tasks. Traditional trajectory-level advantage estimates are too coarse, while recent privileged self-distillation provides denser supervision but lacks a principled way to represent sequential credit. AgentOPSD uses Bayesian belief updates in log-odds space to reweight sparse rewards into turn-level credit signals, identifying pivotal turns via marginal belief revision.

## [OPERA Framework Reduces False Positives in Autonomous Optical Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05990) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OPERA introduces an operator-residual feedback framework for autonomous optical experiments, representing actions as optical operators and evaluating outcomes via physically interpretable residuals. This approach reduced score-only feedback failures from 23.6–39.0% to 0.9–1.9% across three optical tasks. This framework addresses a critical mismatch between agent scores and physical success in autonomous experimentation, potentially improving reliability and efficiency in AI-driven scientific discovery. It could accelerate research in optics and other experimental sciences by reducing wasted resources and false conclusions. The framework was validated across three optical tasks, and protocols selected in digital twins were successfully transferred to three physical optical instruments. Repeated experiments showed a lower projection budget in structured-light reconstruction, indicating improved experimental efficiency.

## [Blueprint for Economic World Models with Six-Level Capability Ladder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06020) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a systems blueprint for building Economic World Models (EWMs) as generative engines, introducing a six-level capability ladder from fixed rule-based agent worlds to sim-to-real economic twins. It surveys existing literature and identifies that most work is concentrated in lower levels, with self-evolving agents and validated economic mechanisms remaining rare. This blueprint provides a structured roadmap for developing high-fidelity economic simulation environments, which could serve as sandboxes for human decision-makers and as training, planning, evaluation, and safety substrates for AI agents. By highlighting research gaps, it aims to accelerate progress in the next generation of economic simulation systems, potentially impacting fields like AI safety, policy-making, and multi-agent systems. The six-level capability ladder includes: fixed rule-based agent worlds, adaptive and LLM-based agent worlds, self-evolving agents, evolving institutional worlds, and sim-to-real economic twins aligned with real observations. The paper also releases a curated paper list and related resources to support future research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Economic World Models (EWMs) are generative economic models that simulate how economies evolve from within by modeling heterogeneous agents, their beliefs and actions, and market and institutional mechanisms. The concept builds on world models in AI, which are internal representations of an environment, and applies them to economics. The six-level capability ladder is a framework to categorize the sophistication of such models, from simple rule-based simulations to fully calibrated digital twins of real economies.

## [InvestLogicBench: A Process-Native Benchmark for Personalized Financial Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06108) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces InvestLogicBench, a large-scale benchmark containing 201,247 documented decisions from 151 real-world investors, designed to evaluate LLMs on personalized investment logic using a P→E→R→D→O trace (Profile, Events, Reasoning, Decision, Outcome). This benchmark addresses a critical gap in financial LLM evaluation by moving beyond static QA and profit/loss metrics, offering a process-native approach that can reveal whether reasoning is grounded and profile-consistent. It sets a new standard for evaluating personalized, consequential agents, with implications for AI in finance and beyond. The benchmark supports comprehension, profile-conditioned generation, and end-to-end replay, and includes profile construction, point-in-time event binding, structured logic, horizons, outcomes, and post-mortems. Across four leading LLMs, logical plausibility remains near 4/5 while event grounding is only 0.8–2.8/5, exposing polished but weakly grounded reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Investment competence is inherently personalized, as the same market evidence can justify different actions for investors with different goals, horizons, portfolios, and risk boundaries. Traditional evaluation methods for financial LLMs either use static question answering, which omits agency, or terminal profit and loss, which cannot reveal whether a profitable action was grounded, profile-consistent, or merely lucky. InvestLogicBench introduces a process-native benchmark that captures the full decision trace, aiming to provide a more accurate measure of an agent's reasoning quality.

## [Poli-Bias: Measuring LLM Political Bias via Counterfactual Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06123) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Poli-Bias, a counterfactual framework that measures LLM biases in international political conflicts by swapping country identities in paired prompts and decomposing response disparities into five interpretable dimensions. The framework was evaluated across 13 contemporary LLMs, revealing systematic differences in how equivalent actions are described, evaluated, and defended under international law. This work addresses a critical and timely issue of political bias in LLMs, providing a fine-grained auditing tool that can help developers and researchers identify and mitigate unfair treatment based on country identities. It contributes to AI fairness research by offering a systematic, multi-dimensional approach to measuring political even-handedness and sycophancy. Poli-Bias decomposes response disparities into five interpretable dimensions, revealing how and where unequal treatment manifests across diverse geopolitical relationships, legal violations, and reasoning tasks. The evaluation spans 13 LLMs from different families and sizes, establishing the framework's effectiveness in auditing political even-handedness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual frameworks are commonly used in causal inference to estimate the effect of an intervention by comparing observed and hypothetical outcomes. In NLP, such frameworks can be applied to measure bias by systematically altering input features (e.g., country names) and observing changes in model outputs. The Poli-Bias framework builds on this idea, adapting it to the domain of international political conflicts to audit LLM behavior.

## [CIPO: Evidence-Oriented RL Framework to Reduce Confirmation Bias in Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06128) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Contextual Information Policy Optimization (CIPO), a reinforcement learning framework that explicitly aligns search agent policy optimization with external evidence use. CIPO assigns dense, turn-level credit to reasoning actions influenced by retrieved information and combines this with a global outcome reward to preserve answer correctness. This addresses a critical problem in LLM-based search agents: confirmation bias, where agents rely on internal knowledge and use retrieval only to confirm pre-existing conclusions. By promoting evidence-grounded reasoning, CIPO could improve the reliability and efficiency of search agents in knowledge-intensive tasks, impacting fields like question answering and fact-checking. CIPO requires neither human process annotations nor an additional reward model, making it practical to implement. Extensive experiments on seven in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks show that CIPO reduces the prevalence of prior-driven reasoning and achieves excellent performance on most tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Search agents extend large language models (LLMs) by allowing them to retrieve and use external evidence during multi-step reasoning. Traditional reinforcement learning methods for these agents often reward only final-answer correctness or intermediate progress, which can lead to confirmation bias. CIPO directly assesses whether post-retrieval actions are grounded in retrieved evidence, providing a more nuanced training signal.

iARCS is a novel iterative agentic reinforcement learning framework that adapts pretrained 3D scene generators to satisfy natural-language task constraints, improving walkability, reachability, and clearance fidelity. It uses a two-stage strategy: universal-reward pretraining and task-specific fine-tuning with LLM-generated reward programs. This addresses a critical gap between perceptual realism and functional constraints in synthetic 3D scene generation, which is increasingly used for training embodied AI and computer vision models. The framework's ability to improve constraint fidelity while maintaining diversity could make synthetic data more reliable for downstream tasks. The framework uses LLM-generated reward programs that are iteratively refined from training feedback, enabling task-specific constraint optimization. Experiments show improved walkability, reachability, and clearance fidelity, and data generated by iARCS improves a base generator, supporting its value as a practical synthetic data generation tool.

MicroEvo, a novel framework coupling off-the-shelf LLMs with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS), improves Pareto-front quality by up to 36.2% over NSGA-II and achieves 10.6x higher search efficiency in microarchitecture design space exploration. ## [EnvACE: World Rehearsal for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06197) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EnvACE introduces an agentic reinforcement learning method that replaces external environment interaction during training with world rehearsal, where the policy alternates between acting and simulating environment responses. It achieves strong performance across BFCL-v4, tau^2-Bench, VitaBench, and FinMCP-Bench, and enables private rehearsal at test time. This approach could significantly reduce the cost and complexity of training LLM agents for tool use by eliminating the need for costly external simulators or real environments. It offers a new path to scale agent training beyond environmental constraints, potentially accelerating progress in autonomous agent development. EnvACE jointly optimizes both the acting and rehearsal roles end-to-end using task-success rewards, internalizing an agent world model. Controlled studies show consistent improvements across model scales, and test-time private rehearsal yields further gains under a moderate rehearsal budget without additional external interaction.

This is related to model-based reinforcement learning and world models, which have been explored in other AI domains.

## [Causal Audit Reveals Illusion of Visual Tool-Use in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06270) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a causal audit framework to evaluate whether visual tool-use in multimodal LLMs truly benefits reasoning. It finds that such operations often yield marginal or negative gains over direct inference, and introduces a step-level estimand called Visual Evidence Gain to isolate the contribution of each returned observation. This work challenges the prevailing 'thinking-with-images' paradigm by showing that visual tool-use may not be causally effective across a broad range of rollouts. The findings could influence future model design and evaluation, prompting researchers to reconsider when and how to deploy visual tools in multimodal LLMs. The audit operates at three levels: policy (comparing tool-use with direct inference), trajectory (corrupting all observations during rollout), and step (counterfactually replacing one observation under a fixed prefix). Across six representative models and five fine-grained perception benchmarks, they identify two failure modes: 'Calling Without Looking' and 'Looking Without Planning'.

**Background**: Multimodal LLMs often use visual tools like crop-and-zoom to enhance reasoning on high-resolution images. However, these tools add token cost and may not improve accuracy. The paper's causal audit framework helps determine whether the visual evidence returned by these tools actually influences the model's answer, distinguishing between genuine reasoning and spurious shortcuts.

HarnessOpt-Bench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate LLMs' ability to optimize agent harnesses under expensive and stochastic evaluation. It provides a standardized protocol with a trusted execution environment, tested across 5 frontier LLMs and 4 downstream tasks over 111 scored runs. ## [Agentic Retrieval READ Outperforms Dense RAG on Table-Heavy Financial Docs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06305) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces READ (Reliable Embedding-free Agentic Document-search), an agentic retrieval method that replaces black-box top-k retrieval with three deterministic operations: normalized lexical search, structural navigation, and bounded span reads, exposed over the Model Context Protocol. On a 780-page government financial report, READ answers 58.8% of 51 verified questions versus dense retrieval's 15.7%. This work addresses a critical limitation of standard RAG for structured documents, where chunking fails to preserve unit context (e.g., lakh vs. crore), leading to errors of two orders of magnitude. The approach offers a replayable audit trail, which is crucial for financial document processing and regulatory compliance, and could influence future retrieval system design. The paper reports that 86.8% of content lines in the test document are table rows, and a figure inherits its unit from a header a median of 13 lines above, so chunk boundaries often separate numbers from their units. A table-aware chunker fixes the unit problem but still leaves 27-30% of numeric chunks without fiscal-year headers. READ also outperforms an agent with a top-k tool (58.8% vs. 27.5%), indicating the gain comes from the interface, not iteration; however, BM25 is statistically indistinguishable from READ.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) typically chunks documents, embeds chunks, and retrieves top-k nearest neighbors. For table-heavy documents like financial statements, this approach fails because chunking breaks unit context and near-identical figures compete in embedding space. Agentic retrieval uses autonomous agents to make dynamic retrieval decisions, and table-aware chunking preserves row-column relationships. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) standardizes how AI models access external tools and data.

## [TrajDebug: Tracing Error Lifecycles to Identify Critical Failures in Long-Horizon Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06346) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TrajDebug, a framework that traces error lifecycles in long-horizon LLM agent trajectories using multi-granularity history compression and evidence-based error attribution. It also presents TrajErrBench, a benchmark of 486 manually annotated failed trajectories from Tau2Bench and SWE-Bench Pro. This work addresses a critical challenge in LLM-based agentic systems: identifying the earliest error that causes final failure in long trajectories. By providing actionable diagnoses, TrajDebug can improve debugging efficiency and downstream agent success, which is vital for the reliability and adoption of AI agents in complex real-world tasks. TrajDebug uses multi-granularity history compression to manage long contexts and evidence-based error identification to locate errors. It also traces each error's resolution status and terminal impact to perform critical attribution. Experiments show it outperforms existing baselines across diverse agent benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based agents often suffer from cascading errors in long-horizon tasks, where a single early mistake can lead to final failure. Debugging such trajectories is difficult because evidence for judging a step may be scattered across distant instructions and observations, and multiple local errors may exist with different downstream effects. TrajDebug aims to address these challenges by tracing the lifecycle of errors from occurrence to impact.

## [Video Language Models Fail at Simple Event Counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06361) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces trace-grounded parametric profiling to evaluate video language models' event counting, revealing a staged temporal failure where models struggle with high-frequency or long-duration events. Across 2,190 videos, they found that at an 80% reliability threshold, Gemini 3.6 Flash counts persistent state transitions up to 12 events at 0.5 and 1.0 Hz, but shows no reliable positive-count region for transient blinking events. This work shifts video evaluation from aggregate accuracy to detailed diagnostics, providing executable ground truth for timestamp-level assessment. The findings highlight fundamental limitations in current video language models' temporal reasoning, which could guide future model improvements and benchmarking practices. The study uses three controlled tasks: bouncing-ball wall contacts, visual blinks, and categorical state transitions, with varying event count and frequency while holding rendering fixed. Increasing sampling rate boosted Bounce Ball accuracy from 19.6% to 29.3%, but the reported sequence agreed with ground truth only 3.7% of the time, indicating extra frames inflate scores without faithful event recovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Video language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process video and text to answer questions or generate descriptions. Traditional benchmarks use fixed clips that entangle multiple factors, making it hard to isolate specific failures. This paper proposes a new evaluation method that uses executable event traces to provide precise ground truth for event counting, allowing for detailed analysis of temporal reasoning capabilities.

## [Mean-Field Dynamics Model for Chain-of-Thought Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05152) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a mean-field framework to model LLM chain-of-thought reasoning as a guided discovery process on a clue graph, deriving a one-dimensional ODE for the fraction of discovered clues. The model is validated using surprisal-based token identification on teacher-student LLM outputs. This work provides a novel theoretical lens for understanding chain-of-thought reasoning without simplifying model architecture, potentially aiding model optimization and interpretability. It bridges statistical physics concepts with LLM reasoning, offering a new direction for theoretical analysis in the field. The framework uses mean-field approximation to derive an ODE for the fraction of discovered clues, and identifies clue tokens via normalized surprisal from a student LLM on teacher LLM outputs. Experiments show statistical regularities are reproducible within the same dataset and fit the theoretical equation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought reasoning in LLMs involves generating intermediate steps to solve complex problems, but its theoretical underpinnings are not well understood. Mean-field approximation is a technique from statistical physics that simplifies complex systems by averaging interactions, and surprisal measures the unlikelihood of a token given context. This paper applies these concepts to model reasoning as a clue discovery process on a graph.

## [GraphRAG Over-Citation Is Universal, But Impact Varies by Corpus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05153) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new triple-robustness study evaluates RAG systems across embedders, corpora, and judges, running 4,440 main-matrix runs and 1,200 paired faithfulness judgments. It finds that GraphRAG consistently over-cites (11-15 IDs per answer, citation precision 0.12-0.23) across all settings, but the faithfulness impact is corpus-dependent. This study challenges the assumption that GraphRAG's citation issues are uniform, showing that its faithfulness consequences depend on the corpus type. It establishes triple-robustness as a minimum standard for trustworthy RAG architecture claims, which is crucial for researchers and practitioners building reliable retrieval-augmented systems. The study varies embedder (e5-small vs. Azure text-embedding-3-small), corpus (DO-178C typed-edge requirements vs. Wikipedia paragraph chains via MuSiQue), and judge (GPT-5.4 vs. GPT-4.1). Key findings include: faithfulness collapses from 74% to 40% on DO-178C but rises from 42% to 58% on Wikipedia chains; single-judge LLM faithfulness is fragile (self-kappa 0.137 for GPT-5.4); a learned router on dense embeddings achieves macro-F1 0.86 for hop classification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) combines LLMs with external knowledge retrieval to improve answer accuracy and reduce hallucinations. GraphRAG uses graph structures to organize information, which can enhance multi-hop reasoning but may also lead to over-citation. Citation precision measures whether cited sources actually support the generated claims, and faithfulness assesses whether the answer stays true to the retrieved context.

## [New Method Estimates Time Spent on 18,000 Work Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05172) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a principled method to estimate time shares for nearly 18,000 tasks that constitute nearly all U.S. jobs, factoring each task's time into expected frequency and time per instance, with the latter derived from a constraint satisfaction problem based on pairwise comparisons elicited from language models. This addresses a significant gap in the task-based framework of economics, providing a general primitive for research and policy on labor economics and AI impact. The findings show that re-weighting by time reshuffles the ranking of occupations most exposed to AI, which could influence policy decisions and our understanding of technology's impact on work. The method validates estimates by characterizing the solution space of the constraint satisfaction problem and collecting worker data for multiple occupations. Applying the time shares reveals that accounting for share of working time exposed to AI, rather than share of tasks, widens the gap between least and most exposed jobs and reshuffles 11 of the 25 occupations widely reported as most exposed, shifting the top away from clerical work toward analytical roles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: The task-based framework in economics models occupations as bundles of tasks and is the standard lens for understanding how technology affects work. Prior work relied on idiosyncratic or ill-justified task weights, and existing time shares were either based on coarse ONET data not intended for this purpose or estimated via black-box language models. This paper proposes a transparent, principled method to estimate time shares, which are crucial for aggregating task-level effects to occupation-level effects.

## [Survey Reveals Verification Gap in Autonomous AI Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05179) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new survey of 35 AI scientist systems, with full-text coding of 26 entries, reveals that while 83% of runnable systems release code, only 38% release seeds or execution traces and 38% report novelty-verification methods. Notably, among nine closed-loop L4 systems, none demonstrate externally validated in-loop oracles. This survey highlights a critical bottleneck in AI-driven research: the ability to verify claims produced by autonomous agents lags behind their capability to generate research outputs. It underscores the need for standardized verification artifacts and reviewer-facing checklists to ensure reproducibility and trust in AI-generated science. The survey screened 125 candidate works and included 35, coding seven audit dimensions: lifecycle stage, autonomy level, evaluation method, released artifacts, human-in-the-loop points, novelty verification, and result-selection disclosure. The authors contribute a coded corpus, a lifecycle-by-autonomy map, an auditability-gap analysis, and a reviewer-facing reporting checklist.

This position paper argues that many LLM failures stem from evaluating outputs independently and proposes self-consistency as a unifying framework to improve model behavior. This framework could reshape how researchers approach LLM training and evaluation, potentially leading to more reliable and robust models. This paper introduces HatefulStoryPrompts, a benchmark of 330 multi-turn configurations from 55 hateful stories across two languages and three visual styles, and evaluates five frontier text-to-image models over 4,950 attempts. It reveals that all models complete over 80% of the stories, with the strongest reaching 99.0%, and that existing moderation systems frequently miss group-level hateful meaning. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety: group-level hateful narratives in multi-turn visual story generation, which can be cheaply and scalably produced by frontier T2I systems. It highlights the need for safety mechanisms to evolve from per-image moderation to stateful reasoning over interactions and image relationships, impacting the broader AI safety and content moderation ecosystem. The paper also introduces HatefulVisualStory, a human-labeled dataset of 969 hateful image sets and 990 benign controls, and evaluates existing moderation systems, finding that dedicated safety models achieve at most 34.9% recall while a strong vision-language model reaches 67.5%. Proposed defenses include an interaction-aware monitor achieving 97.3% recall for prompt-only sessions and 92.6% when the user supplies the first image, and post-generation methods reaching 80.2%.

## [Marginal Matching Fails to Ensure Style-Class Independence in Factorized Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05243) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that matching the marginal distribution of latent style variables to a Gaussian prior does not guarantee class-conditional independence, allowing style leakage that linear probes can exploit. The authors provide theoretical decomposition and empirical evidence showing near-zero MMD but high linear probe accuracy (74%-100%). This finding challenges a common assumption in factorized generative models, impacting representation learning and model evaluation. It underscores that reporting marginal statistics alone is insufficient to verify style-class independence, which has broad implications for the reliability of such models in downstream tasks. The case-study model and four baselines achieve near-zero global MMD while linear probes recover class labels with 74%-100% accuracy (10% chance level). Four mitigation strategies reduce probe accuracy to 21%-46%, but a post-hoc conditional prior improves class-conditional generation to 0.97 on MNIST and 0.41 on CIFAR-10, while an empirical style bank reaches 0.88 on CIFAR-10.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Factorized generative models decompose data into latent factors, often regularizing a style variable to match a Gaussian prior to encourage independence from class information. Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) is a kernel-based metric used to compare distributions, and linear probes are simple classifiers used to assess what information is linearly accessible in representations. This paper shows that marginal matching via MMD does not constrain class-conditional distributions, leading to style leakage.
