## [Perturbation Sensitivity at Convergence Identifies Spurious Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05419) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method to identify spuriously correlated samples by applying a fixed perturbation to a converged model's inputs, requiring only two forward passes per sample and no group annotations. The approach improves worst-group accuracy on Waterbirds from 57.3% to 80.8%, compared to 85.8% with ground-truth labels. This work addresses a critical challenge in machine learning: identifying spurious correlations without group annotations, which is essential for improving model robustness and fairness. The simplicity of the method (two forward passes) makes it practical for real-world applications, potentially enabling more reliable models in high-stakes domains. The method exploits the observation that samples consistent with spurious correlations are classified by a shared rule and are more robust to perturbations, while non-spurious samples are fit through input-specific configurations and are more fragile. It avoids the need for early-stopping epochs or group-labeled validation data, unlike previous approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Empirical risk minimization (ERM) models trained on data with spurious correlations often achieve high average accuracy but fail on minority subpopulations. This paper shows that a usable signal exists after convergence, when loss no longer distinguishes the two populations.

## [Matrix Zonotopic Attention: Context-Adaptive Value Projection for Set Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05472) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Matrix Zonotopic Attention (MZAttn), which replaces the fixed value projection in standard multi-head attention with a context-adaptive matrix-zonotope family. It also provides a depth-separation analysis and a new complexity measure called Transformation Degrees of Freedom (TDOF), showing that a single MZAttn layer can represent targets that require depth proportional to TDOF in standard attention. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of standard attention—its input-independent value projection—by introducing a context-adaptive mechanism that can improve the efficiency and expressiveness of set transformers. The theoretical analysis and proposed method could influence future attention designs and benefit applications involving permutation-invariant set prediction tasks. MZAttn reduces to standard multi-head attention at initialization and preserves permutation equivariance. Experiments on set-prediction tasks show that the advantage is selective: it appears on targets that depend on the input set in a high-rank, sparsely combinatorial way, but is small on aggregate-statistic targets where parameter-matched standard attention is already competitive.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Set transformers are neural architectures designed to process sets of elements in a permutation-invariant manner, often using attention mechanisms. Standard multi-head attention uses a fixed, input-independent value projection, which may limit its ability to adapt to different input sets. The paper introduces TDOF to quantify the complexity of target operators and proposes MZAttn to overcome the rigidity of standard attention.

## [Autogrammar: Learning CFGs for DSLs via Declarative Agentic Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05493) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Autogrammar, an agent that automatically learns context-free grammars for domain-specific languages from documentation and execution data, formalized as a Kripke structure with linear temporal logic constraints. It achieves near-perfect precision on unseen data across three DSLs and improves end-to-end LM performance on eight out of ten real tasks. Grammar-constrained decoding is crucial for ensuring syntactically valid outputs from language models when interacting with external services, but obtaining grammars for third-party DSLs is a bottleneck. Autogrammar addresses this by automating grammar learning, potentially enabling broader adoption of GCD and improving reliability of LLM-based tools. The agent is evaluated on three DSLs: Amazon CloudWatch Logs Insights, Dynatrace Query Language, and Datadog Search Syntax. Temporal restrictions reduce execution time by 3.8x without statistically significant precision loss, and execution data is crucial while documentation is dispensable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) is a technique that filters an LLM's vocabulary at each generation step using a formal grammar, ensuring the output follows a given structure. A Kripke structure is a graph-based model used in model checking to represent system states and transitions, and linear temporal logic (LTL) allows specifying constraints over time. Autogrammar uses these formalisms to declaratively control an agent that learns grammars.

## [PoisonedEvolution: Trajectory Poisoning Attack on Self-Evolving Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05563) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PoisonedEvolution, a trajectory-poisoning attack that embeds malicious behaviors into self-evolving skill (SES) systems. It achieves a 91.0% success rate across six LLM evolvers in SkillClaw and 61.5% on Trace2Skill at 10% attacker support. This attack exposes a critical security vulnerability in self-evolving agent systems, where untrusted experience can be promoted into trusted instructions. It highlights the need for robust security measures in LLM agent pipelines, affecting developers and users of such systems. The attack requires three conditions: Inclusion, Evolution Attribution, and Realization, with attribution being the main bottleneck. Ablations show that recurring support, causal framing, and domain-aligned encoding are key success factors; three consistent attacker records suffice in a 30-record batch.

**Background**: Self-evolving skill (SES) systems distill agent trajectories into persistent skills, allowing agents to improve over time. ## [LC-GRPO: Langevin Correction Bridges Train-Inference Gap in Flow-Based RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05600) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces LC-GRPO, a flow-based GRPO framework that applies a Langevin correction to each ODE Euler step during training, aligning stochastic rollouts with deterministic test-time ODE sampling. This method eliminates the need for a separate score model by recovering the score directly from the flow velocity. This work addresses a critical mismatch in reinforcement learning for flow-based generative models, where SDE-based training rollouts often become blurry and diverge from ODE-based inference. By narrowing this gap, LC-GRPO improves reward optimization and generation quality, potentially enhancing the efficiency and reliability of RL fine-tuning for text-to-image and text-to-video models. The method theoretically proves that one Langevin correction step reduces the Wasserstein error of an imperfect ODE Euler step, and at matched randomness, it can be more accurate than standard Euler-Maruyama discretization of the reverse SDE. Experiments on SD3.5-Medium, FLUX.1-Dev, and HunyuanVideo show consistent improvements in reward optimization while preserving generation quality.

Existing GRPO methods replace the ODE with an SDE during training, but finite-step discretizations of ODE and SDE can differ, causing a train-inference gap. Langevin dynamics is a technique used in score-based generative models to sample from a distribution using score estimates.

SkillZip introduces a novel framework that compresses agent skill libraries at the section-level graph abstraction while preserving procedural contracts, achieving up to 3.46x compression with 99.2% dependency preservation and 98.7% verifier reachability. It outperforms the strongest baseline by up to 12.2 points on technical and embodied agent benchmarks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in scaling LLM-based agents: efficiently reusing procedural knowledge under limited context budgets. By enabling reliable, contract-preserving compression, SkillZip could significantly improve the performance and scalability of agent systems across various domains. SkillZip rewrites recurring contract-valid motifs into reversible ported macros while preserving boundary signatures, dependency closure, verifier reachability, and source-level expansion. It also includes ReZip, a mechanism to integrate new skills and revise risky macros using execution evidence, and demonstrates robust retrieval across skill libraries ranging from 200 to 100K skills.

The paper introduces Relay, a training-free framework that adaptively hands off between cheap and strong LLMs based on population state, shifting budget allocation from individual calls to evolving populations. It achieves the highest mean score in 11 of 12 settings across four benchmarks and three budgets. This addresses the practical and timely problem of cost-efficient LLM-driven evolution, which is crucial for program search and algorithm discovery. By organizing budget allocation around populations rather than individual calls, it offers a more efficient paradigm that could reduce costs while maintaining performance, benefiting researchers and practitioners in AI/ML. Relay uses a bandit scheduler to allocate short blocks to a cheap model for exploring multiple trajectories, with Relay Gain as the reward to decide handoff. The curated candidates initialize a shared strong model population for refinement, and the framework is training-free.

## [Answer First, Reason Later: Commitment Order in Diffusion LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05687) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that masked diffusion language models (dLLMs) fail on reasoning tasks because they commit to final answers too early due to reachability constraints, not termination beliefs. The authors show that chain-of-thought prompting only helps under ordered commitment, and propose a frontier-gated commitment intervention that recovers performance while preserving parallel decoding. This finding challenges a core claimed advantage of diffusion LLMs—flexible token commitment—and identifies it as a failure axis for reasoning. It has significant implications for the design of diffusion-based language models and provides a causal mechanism that can guide future improvements. The study uses LLaDA-8B on GSM8K, finding that unconstrained decoding commits the final answer at 15-24% of the trajectory and collapses to answer-only outputs on up to 90% of problems. A 2x2 prompt-decoder design shows chain-of-thought helps only under ordered commitment (interaction +34.8 percentage points), and the proposed frontier-gated commitment recovers the gap (0.528 to 0.852) while preserving up to 4x parallel decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising masked tokens, allowing tokens to be committed in any order, unlike autoregressive models that generate left-to-right. This flexibility is often marketed as an advantage, but this paper shows it can be detrimental for reasoning tasks. The study introduces concepts like 'reachability' (whether the sampler can act on termination beliefs at distant positions) and 'commitment order' to explain the failure mode.

This paper introduces Cross-Variable Loss (CvLoss), a plug-in structural regularizer that constrains forecast residuals on a cross-variable graph, addressing the objective gap in the Direct Forecasting (DF) paradigm for multivariate time series forecasting. CvLoss explicitly models dependencies among future values, which are often overlooked, and consistently improves competitive forecasting models, potentially advancing the state of the art in multivariate time series forecasting. CvLoss penalizes inconsistent edge-wise residual differences over forecast patches, encouraging consistency across both synchronous and asynchronous interactions. It is compatible with various forecasting backbones and outperforms representative learning objectives in experiments.

## [GROM: Gradient-Free One-Shot Machine Unlearning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05783) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GROM introduces a one-shot machine unlearning method that uses a closed-form ridge-regularized least-squares solution to remove knowledge from LLMs, eliminating iterative fine-tuning and backpropagation. It applies weight edits in seconds and achieves state-of-the-art forgetting-utility trade-offs on benchmarks like TOFU and WMDP. This is significant because it addresses the computational cost and limitations of gradient-based unlearning, offering a faster and more robust alternative for LLM safety and privacy. It could enable practical unlearning in resource-constrained settings and improve resistance to quantization attacks that recover supposedly forgotten content. GROM formulates unlearning as a ridge-regularized least-squares problem, deriving a closed-form additive update for targeted weight matrices that suppresses unwanted content while preserving retained data behavior. It is computed from gradient-free forward passes alone, with no backpropagation or iteration, and withstands low-bit quantization attacks that recover content from gradient-based baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove specific knowledge or data from trained models without full retraining, which is crucial for privacy and safety in LLMs. Current methods often rely on iterative fine-tuning, which is computationally expensive and may only hide knowledge rather than truly remove it. Ridge regression is a regularized least-squares technique that provides closed-form solutions, and LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that reduces trainable parameters.

MACRO introduces a Markov chain-based framework for task-specific routing of transformer layers in LLMs, enabling skip, repeat, and residual operations without weight updates. It achieves a +5.0% average accuracy improvement over unrouted baselines and outperforms Dr. LLM by +7.2% while reducing route-search time 9.4x. This work addresses the inefficiency of sequential layer execution in LLMs by enabling dynamic routing without weight modification, which could lead to more efficient inference and better performance on reasoning tasks. It is particularly beneficial for smaller models and offers a practical alternative to existing routing methods that require expensive search or weight updates. MACRO models layer routing as a context-dependent Markov policy conditioned on layer indices, computation budget phases, directional displacements, and operator context. The route distribution is updated via training data feedback and decoded using a top-k Viterbi algorithm to isolate high-probability candidate programs.

Researchers introduced FormBharo, a voice agent that combines large language models with rule-based validation to fill structured forms over phone calls, and piloted it with ARMMAN to enroll low-income Hindi-speaking mothers in maternal health programs. They also released FormVoiceAgentBench, a benchmark with 3,760 multi-turn conversation tests across 960 simulated calls. This work addresses a critical accessibility gap in India, where many social benefits require form filling but a large population is illiterate. Notably, component performance does not predict end-to-end performance; GPT-5.5 leads turn-level extraction accuracy on reference transcripts (99.8%) but ranks lower on form completion, and no single model is best across accuracy, cost, and latency, so a Pareto-based weighted-sum scalarization is used for model selection.

## [SiPE: Syntax-Informed Positional Embeddings Improve Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06111) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SiPE (Syntax-informed Positional Embeddings), a method that learns a lightweight syntactic prior from dependency parses during pretraining and injects it into positional embeddings across absolute, relative, and rotary PE families, for both encoders and decoders. SiPE improves SyntaxGym by up to 10.3% and reduces perplexity by 9.0% over a base model without syntactic supervision, while also boosting GLUE scores by up to 8.2%. This work addresses a known limitation of Transformers—their positional embeddings are largely agnostic to syntactic structure—by providing a method that improves both syntactic generalization and general language understanding without increasing inference cost. It establishes a new Pareto frontier between syntactic supervision and inference cost, making syntax-informed modeling more practical for real-world applications. The optimal injection point depends on the architecture: for autoregressive decoders using relative PE, the prior is strongest when coupled multiplicatively with the relative-position term of the attention score; for encoders, it is best added directly to the input embeddings. SiPE conditions on a single parse, unlike existing syntactic language models that marginalize over many parses at inference or discard syntax at runtime.

**Background**: Transformers use positional embeddings to encode token order and distance, but these are typically agnostic to syntactic structure. Dependency parsing is a technique in NLP that analyzes the grammatical structure of a sentence, establishing relationships between words. SiPE leverages this syntactic information to enhance positional embeddings, improving model performance on both syntactic and semantic tasks.

## [LLM Dialogues Reduce Belief in Unfolding Conspiracy Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06151) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study demonstrates that multi-turn conversations with a large language model (LLM) can significantly reduce belief in conspiracy theories as they emerge, tested in the aftermath of the July 2024 Trump assassination attempt and the September 2025 Charlie Kirk assassination. The effects persisted, reducing belief in different conspiracies one to two months later. This research offers a scalable, evidence-based intervention for combating misinformation and conspiracy beliefs in real time, which could be crucial for public health, democracy, and social cohesion. It also opens new avenues for using AI in social psychology and misinformation mitigation. The study involved two experiments with U.S. adults (N=472 and N=1035) who held conspiratorial views about the crisis events. Participants in the LLM treatment showed significantly reduced conspiracy beliefs compared to control groups that discussed an irrelevant topic or viewed a static fact sheet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Conspiracy theories often proliferate after major events, posing societal challenges. This study leverages large language models, which can engage in natural, multi-turn dialogues, to deliver personalized, cognitive interventions that challenge conspiratorial beliefs in a conversational manner, potentially offering a more effective approach than static information.

## [GSE: Global Skill Evolution Framework for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06153) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces GSE, a globalized skill evolution framework for LLM agents that jointly optimizes skill compatibility and generalization using a Skill Relation Graph and cluster-based consolidation. Evaluated on bug-revealing test generation and false-positive filtering, GSE improves precision and recall by up to 34.1% and 180.0% respectively over existing techniques. This framework addresses a critical limitation in current skill evolution methods, which often produce overfitted updates that fail to generalize. By improving the generalization of coding agents, GSE could enhance the reliability and efficiency of automated software engineering, benefiting both research and industrial applications. GSE maintains a Skill Relation Graph (SRG) to model inter-skill relationships and uses cluster-based consolidation to abstract reusable capabilities. It also employs replay-driven verification to prevent overfitting and behavioral regressions, and was tested on OpenHands and mini-SWE-agent, with an internal industrial agent showing a 61.4% F1-score improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Automated skill evolution allows LLM agents to improve without retraining, but existing methods treat it as local updates, ignoring skill relationships and causing overfitting. GSE introduces a global perspective by modeling skill relationships and consolidating clusters, which helps agents generalize across tasks. This is relevant to the broader trend of making AI agents more autonomous and adaptable in software engineering.

## [Zero-Shot VLMs Fail Visual Grounding Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06154) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06154) reveals that many zero-shot vision-language controllers do not truly ground decisions in visual input, as constant policies and image-invariant models can outperform them. The study uses an input-ablation battery across nine direct-action models, six structured local VLMs, and an exploratory VLM-MPC hierarchy, analyzing 32,874 scored calls. This negative result challenges the assumption that VLMs are capable zero-shot controllers, with significant implications for safe deployment in robotics and autonomous systems. It highlights the need for rigorous grounding evaluation before relying on VLMs in safety-critical applications. The direct-control results are largely negative: a constant-SLOW policy outperforms a scripted geometric controller, several models are image-invariant or nearly constant, and models fail to transform LEFT and RIGHT under reflection. However, an image-only deterministic positive control estimates lead gap with 0.090 m MAE and exact mirror equivariance, confirming the stimuli carry sufficient visual information; failures are modular, not universal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used as zero-shot controllers, but successful trajectories do not necessarily prove that decisions are grounded in visual input. Simulator dynamics and conservative action priors can produce favorable scores without meaningful perception. Input ablation is a method that systematically perturbs or masks input variables to measure their impact on model output, used here to test visual grounding.

## [AI Benchmarks Miss Modality, Search, Citations, Impacting Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06202) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study audits assumptions in LLM benchmarking, revealing that access modality (chat UI vs. API), web search, and citation grounding significantly affect model behavior and safety evaluations. Using 401 prompts from BBQ and SafetyBench, the study collected 4,812 responses and found accuracy drops up to 8 percentage points with web search enabled, and inconsistent responses in up to 21% of prompts. This matters because current AI safety evaluations often rely on simple accuracy metrics from a single modality, which can obscure important behavioral variations relevant to deployment readiness. The findings call for more comprehensive evaluation practices that account for modality, multi-run consistency, search conditions, and response-level behaviors, directly impacting how AI systems are assessed for safety. The study used a stratified sample of 401 prompts from BBQ and SafetyBench, with three repeated runs per prompt, totaling 4,812 responses. It evaluated response consistency, text similarity, citation grounding, and abstention behavior, finding that chat UI responses were less accurate than API responses without search, and enabling search reversed modality performance trends for one benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM benchmarks are commonly used to evaluate model safety and reliability, but most evaluations assume a single access modality (e.g., API), perform one run per prompt, and report accuracy as the primary metric. However, deployed AI systems often operate under different conditions, such as chat interfaces and web search, which can alter model behavior. Citation grounding refers to constraining model outputs to facts from retrieved sources with explicit citations, while abstention behavior is the model's ability to refuse answering when uncertain. These factors are often overlooked in standard evaluations, leading to an incomplete picture of model performance in real-world scenarios.

This paper introduces a tri-axial framework (When, How, Where) to characterize the evolution of continual learning from parameter-centric methods to system-level adaptation, covering emerging paradigms like on-policy learning and test-time training. This framework provides a unified perspective that could guide future research in continual learning, helping researchers navigate the shift toward more holistic, system-level approaches that extend beyond traditional weight updates. The How dimension includes off-policy, on-policy, and beyond-gradient optimization; the When dimension spans pre-training, post-training, and inference-time; the Where dimension distinguishes internal parameter updates from external structural constraints. The paper surveys representative methods and discusses challenges and future directions.

## [Holonic Digital Twin Network Framework for Physical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06227) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a novel holonic digital twin network (HDT-Nets) framework that enables real-time physical AI inference by having agents actively reason about their environment, rather than passively mirroring physical assets. It integrates causal Markov blankets, active inference, category theory, and integrated information theory to coordinate agents across sensing, communication, and control. This framework addresses a critical gap in current AI systems, which often fail in physical applications due to unreliable world models and inability to generalize. It could significantly impact robotics, autonomous systems, and network design by enabling more robust and adaptive physical AI coordination. The framework uses causal Markov blankets spanning sensing, communication, and control to determine which agents must coordinate, and active inference minimizes expected free energy while deciding which beliefs to transmit based on cognitive value. Category theory ensures semantic preservation across heterogeneous agents, and integrated information theory quantifies when collective intelligence exceeds independent operation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Physical AI refers to AI systems embedded in physical entities like robots and vehicles, which must operate under real-world physical laws. Digital twins are virtual replicas of physical assets, and holonic systems are hierarchical structures where each part is both a whole and a part of a larger whole. This framework combines these concepts to address the limitations of current AI in physical environments.

## [BaKron: Efficient Kronecker-Factored Hessian Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06291) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BaKron introduces an efficient solver for two-sided adaptive rounding in neural network quantization, reducing computational complexity from O(m^2 n^2) to O(mn(m+n)) for an m×n weight matrix. It combines anti-diagonal parallelism with a recursive divide-and-conquer construction, achieving cubic scaling similar to GPTQ while leveraging richer Kronecker-factored Hessian information. This advancement makes two-sided curvature-aware quantization computationally feasible for large models, potentially enabling broader adoption of more accurate post-training quantization methods. It addresses a key bottleneck in model compression, benefiting researchers and practitioners working on efficient inference and deployment of deep neural networks. BaKron is modular with respect to both the base quantizer and the Hessian estimator, allowing flexibility in different settings. The paper also provides practical benchmarks, explores various Hessian choices, and proposes an efficient technique to compute these Hessians, with experimental validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Quantization reduces model size and speeds up inference by representing weights with lower precision. GPTQ-style adaptive rounding uses one-sided information from input activations, while two-sided Kronecker-factored Hessian approximations capture correlations across output coordinates but are computationally expensive. BaKron builds on prior work like BoA and YAQA to make two-sided adaptive rounding efficient.

## [FLAIR Super-Resolution May Erase Small White-Matter Lesions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06311) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study systematically evaluated whether FLAIR super-resolution (SR) methods erase or hallucinate small white-matter lesions, finding that the dominant effect is erasure of small real lesions, not hallucination, and that erasure increases with slice thickness. The study used 29 ADNI subjects with expert segmentations and tested multiple SR methods including implicit neural representation (INR), ECLARE, and cubic interpolation. This matters because super-resolution is widely used in clinical imaging, and if it erases small lesions, it could lead to missed diagnoses of cerebrovascular or neurodegenerative conditions. The findings highlight the need for SR evaluation standards that include lesion preservation metrics, impacting both AI developers and clinical radiologists. The study used 1-mm isotropic high-resolution FLAIR scans from 29 ADNI participants, degraded to simulate 3-mm and 5-mm through-plane acquisitions. Four WMH segmentation methods were considered, but the analysis focused on MARS-WMH, the most sensitive to small lesions on HR; ECLARE recovered small lesion signal best at both thicknesses, while the INR was no better than cubic interpolation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: White matter hyperintensities (WMH) are bright regions on FLAIR scans associated with cerebrovascular pathology and neurodegeneration. FLAIR is often acquired with thick slices, leading to poor through-plane resolution, and super-resolution (SR) is used to recover isotropic volumes. However, SR may introduce artifacts such as erasing real lesions or hallucinating absent ones, which could affect downstream segmentation and clinical diagnosis.

## [AV-AIVAT: 74x Cheaper Agent Evaluation with Certified Anytime-Valid Stopping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06362) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AV-AIVAT, a method that combines AIVAT variance reduction with confidence sequences to enable certified anytime-valid stopping in agent evaluation. In poker experiments, it reduces the number of hands needed by up to 74x compared to raw outcomes. This work addresses a practical problem in AI evaluation: determining when to stop comparing agents without invalidating statistical guarantees. It could significantly reduce costs in domains where evaluation requires expensive game plays or model inferences, and its anytime-valid framework offers rigorous guarantees for continuous monitoring. The method uses Asymptotic CS (AsympCS) for asymptotic screening and Empirical-Bernstein CS (EB-CS) for exact finite-sample certification, requiring an independently justified bound on corrected payoffs. The paper establishes such a bound for Leduc hold'em and characterizes a width floor set by the CS's bet cap and that bound, with HUNL EB-CS runs showing a median 1.37x stopping-time ratio.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: AIVAT (Action-Informed Value Assessment Tool) is a variance reduction technique for imperfect-information games that uses conditional mean-zero corrections to reduce variance. Confidence sequences are anytime-valid confidence intervals that remain valid under optional stopping, unlike fixed-time confidence intervals. This work combines these two ideas to create an efficient and statistically sound stopping rule for agent evaluation.

## [Selective Context Preference Optimization Teaches LLMs When to Trust](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06377) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MIST, a human-annotated benchmark with four matched conditions (clean, misleading, correct-context, irrelevant-context), and SC2W, a paired metric that measures how often misleading context flips a correct answer to wrong. It also proposes SCOPE, a DPO-based method that balances preference pairs across all four conditions, roughly halving SC2W on open-source models without sacrificing accuracy on clean or relevant contexts. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM robustness: models that resist all context are useless when context is helpful. By framing the problem as selective trust, it provides a benchmark and method that could lead to more reliable and useful language models in real-world applications where context quality varies. SCOPE leaves the DPO objective unchanged; the novelty lies in how preference pairs are constructed and balanced across all four conditions, not just misleading ones. The benchmark study shows universal susceptibility to misleading signals across frontier models (GPT-5.5, Claude, Gemini) and open-weight models, highlighting the breadth of the problem.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Language models increasingly rely on external context such as retrieved passages or tool outputs, but a single misleading signal can flip a correct answer to wrong. Traditional robustness training often makes models ignore all context, which is undesirable when context is trustworthy. Selective trust aims to balance robustness and utility by teaching models when to trust context. DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) is a preference optimization method that aligns models with human preferences without reinforcement learning.

## [Test-Time Scaling Fails to Boost Knowledge-Intensive Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06861) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study evaluating 14 reasoning models reveals that increasing test-time computation does not consistently improve accuracy on closed-book knowledge-intensive tasks and often leads to more hallucinations. The paper also provides an information-theoretic perspective explaining why compute-only scaling cannot introduce new information about the ground-truth answer. This finding challenges the prevailing assumption that more reasoning compute always helps, which is critical as AI systems are increasingly deployed in knowledge-intensive domains like medicine and law. It highlights the need for alternative approaches, such as retrieval augmentation or external knowledge access, to improve factual accuracy. The study tested 14 reasoning models under various test-time scaling strategies, finding that hallucination rate changes are largely driven by the model's willingness to answer, as longer reasoning encourages more attempts, many incorrect. It also observed patterns consistent with confirmation bias, where extended reasoning reinforces early incorrect beliefs with fabricated details.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling refers to techniques that allocate additional compute during inference, such as generating longer reasoning chains, to improve performance on complex tasks. Reasoning models, like OpenAI's o1 or DeepSeek-R1, use these techniques to 'think' more before answering. However, this study shows that for tasks requiring factual knowledge, simply thinking longer does not help and can even hurt accuracy.

SimMOF is a new large language model (LLM)-based multi-agent framework that automates end-to-end metal-organic framework (MOF) simulation workflows from natural language queries. It represents a timely application of AI agents in materials science, with broad implications for high-throughput screening and data-driven research. SimMOF is introduced as an arXiv preprint (arXiv:2603.29152v3) and has not yet undergone peer review. The framework's multi-agent design mimics the iterative, decision-driven behavior of human researchers, enabling adaptive and cognitively autonomous workflows.

## [Axiomatic Benchmark for Evaluating Scientific Novelty Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15145) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the first axiomatic benchmark for evaluating scientific novelty metrics, using controlled pool manipulations and three axioms without requiring explicit novelty labels. It tests ten systems across three AI domains and shows that surface redundancy is largely solved while conceptual redundancy is not. This benchmark provides a reliable, label-free method to compare novelty metrics, which is crucial for AI scientists to avoid wasting compute on already-explored ideas. It highlights the limitations of current metrics and suggests combining embedding and LLM-based approaches for better novelty evaluation. The benchmark defines three axioms: redundancy (scores fall as pool covers more content), relevance (scores rise as pool loses relevance), and temporality (scores fall as pool moves later in time). It also shows that ICLR reviewer novelty scores are entangled with quality, indicating human novelty judgments are confounded.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific novelty metrics are used to assess whether a paper introduces new ideas, but they are often validated against noisy signals like citation counts or peer review scores. The challenge is the lack of ground-truth novelty labels. This benchmark addresses this by using controlled pool manipulations and axioms, providing a principled way to evaluate metrics without labels.

## [EvoMap Study Reveals Scalability vs. Reusability Trade-offs in A2A Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25815) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first large-scale empirical study of EvoMap, a decentralized agent-to-agent (A2A) collaboration network, analyzing over 1.5 million assets and 128,000 agents. The study reveals that design choices prioritizing scalable growth introduce significant trade-offs in asset reusability, evolution, and auditability. These findings have important implications for the emerging field of multi-agent AI systems and decentralized AI ecosystems. They highlight that future A2A networks cannot rely solely on unverified self-reporting and need mechanisms that balance open participation with verifiable execution and trustworthy evaluation. The study found that 98% of assets are never reused, and rewards are highly concentrated among a small fraction of agents. Additionally, the GDI scoring algorithm is flawed because it relies on unverified self-reported metadata, and over 84% of approved assets bypass quality checks using vacuous tests like console.log().

The GDI algorithm is used to score and rank assets, but its reliance on self-reported data makes it susceptible to manipulation.

The paper introduces Theo, an agentic autoformalization framework that uses general coding LLMs to translate research-level mathematics into machine-checked Lean 4 code. It dynamically extends type definitions for concepts beyond existing libraries and validates them via an Auxiliary Lemma technique. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in formal verification by enabling the autoformalization of cutting-edge research mathematics, which typically relies on concepts not covered by existing libraries. ## [TRACE-RealWorld: Auditable Consistency Contract for World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21910) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TRACE-RealWorld (TRW) introduces the first commitment-level consistency contract for world models, treating predicted state as materialized views with adaptive refresh and SagaLLM compensation, and proves a conditional consistency guarantee. This work addresses the critical problem of state drift in world models, offering a formal framework to make them auditable and reliable for AI planning. It could significantly impact fields like robotics and autonomous systems where accurate world prediction is essential. TRW uses typed, calibrated claims for consequence-conditioned freshness and priced verification, and adaptive refresh generalizes dual-Kalman synchronization. The implementation in Flood-SAR shows adaptive refresh reduces stale execution but does not dominate fixed refresh on all metrics, and localized repair reduces repair work by 9.56 units per mission and restoration latency by 80.7 seconds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: World models are internal representations that agents use to predict physical state for planning. Materialized views are database concepts where query results are stored and refreshed; applying this to world models helps manage consistency. Dual-Kalman filtering is a technique for state estimation and synchronization, often used in control systems.

## [Property-Driven Causal Abstractions for MDPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26787) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel property-driven causal abstraction technique for factored Markov Decision Processes (MDPs) that leverages causal relations over state variable predicates to reduce state space while preserving properties. The approach is validated across MDPs, interval MDPs, and stochastic games, achieving near-optimal policies on standard benchmarks. This work addresses the scalability challenge in MDP reasoning by providing a principled abstraction method that retains key characteristics of the original model. It has potential implications for formal verification and AI planning, enabling more efficient analysis of large-scale decision-making problems. The method identifies states that share the same reasons for fulfilling or violating a given abstraction property, using causal relations over state variable predicates. The paper provides theoretical and empirical comparisons across different model types, showing that the causal abstractions often generalize to related large-scale MDP models.

This paper introduces a causality-based approach to abstraction, which is a novel direction compared to traditional methods.

## [Flow-Matching Uncertainty Proxy for VLA Failure Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27933) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a geometric interpretation of uncertainty in flow matching, proposing a cost-free proxy called denoising acceleration (accel) that measures the bending of the denoising trajectory from a single forward pass, without additional training or resampling. The method is demonstrated to be effective for online failure detection in flow-based VLA models. This work addresses a critical safety issue in embodied AI by providing a cost-effective uncertainty proxy for flow-based VLA models, enabling reliable deployment in real-time control. It matches or outperforms costly baselines, potentially improving trust and safety in autonomous systems. The proxy, accel, is theoretically and empirically shown to be a faithful indicator of FM uncertainty, and it identifies failing rollouts before termination under realistic deployment budgets. Code and demos are available at the provided GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling paradigm that learns a velocity field to transport samples from noise to data, and it has become a popular action head for embodied models. However, as a conditional generative model, it does not explicitly expose uncertainty, which is crucial for safe deployment. Existing uncertainty estimation methods often require extra training, high computational overhead, or lack generalization.

OSReward introduces a benchmark to evaluate vision-language model (VLM) judges on computer-use agent (CUA) trajectories, with variants OSReward-Hard and OSReward-Multi for challenging and fine-grained scoring. The authors also release OS-Shepherd-100K, a corpus of reasoning-annotated trajectory judgments, and train OS-Shepherd (9B and 35B) reward models that match commercial judges at 30-60x lower cost. The benchmark includes trajectories from diverse agent backbones across web, Windows, Ubuntu, and mobile platforms, labeled with ground-truth verdicts via multi-stage human annotation. The evaluation finds that even state-of-the-art VLMs exhibit a leniency bias, mislabeling failed runs as successes, and that affordable open models lag behind, motivating the development of OS-Shepherd.

## [Hybrid GUI-MCP Agents: Tool Availability Doesn't Guarantee Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper on arXiv (2608.03327) reveals that in hybrid GUI-MCP computer-use agents, having tools available does not consistently improve performance; the same MCP tools boosted a reasoning model by +4.0pp but degraded a non-reasoning model by -5.9pp on OSWorld-MCP. The authors introduce the 'adoption gap'—models underuse tools—and show that dense tool bonuses via reinforcement learning can increase tool adoption but not held-out accuracy. This finding challenges the assumption that simply providing tools to AI agents improves their performance, highlighting the critical role of tool-decision behavior. It has implications for designing more effective hybrid agents that can intelligently choose and integrate tools, potentially improving efficiency and accuracy in real-world computer-use tasks. The study used the OSWorld-MCP benchmark with 309 tasks and found that the non-reasoning policy ignored, misnamed, or falsely terminated around tools, while the reasoning model called tools on only 55/309 tasks (23.9% of tool-reachable ones). Dense tool bonuses raised spreadsheet adoption from 0.03 to 0.33 but did not improve held-out accuracy; compressing the agent by dropping redundant screenshots cut input tokens by about a third, reaching 37.8% accuracy at 53% of the input cost.

## [EviGraph: Evidence-Guided Autonomous Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04738) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EviGraph introduces a novel autonomous research framework that represents the research process as a typed evidence graph with Problem, Gap, Hypothesis, Experiment, Finding, and Claim nodes, and uses it as the operational state to maintain claim-evidence consistency. It outperforms baselines on ARC-Bench-ML and NanoResearch-20, improving Claim Support Rate by 40.19% and achieving 87.73% Experimental Data Consistency. This work addresses a critical limitation in autonomous research agents—unsupported claims and inconsistencies—by making evidence-state maintenance explicit, which could significantly improve the reliability of AI-generated scientific outputs. It offers a new architectural pattern that may influence future research-agent designs and enhance trust in automated scientific discovery. EviGraph inspects evidence chains for missing dependencies, semantic misalignment, and result-claim inconsistencies, then localizes the earliest weak node and regenerates its affected downstream subgraph. Graph checkpointing prevents unsuccessful repairs from corrupting previously validated evidence, and manuscripts are generated only after every retained claim is grounded in a validated evidence chain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous research agents are AI systems that can generate hypotheses, execute experiments, and draft manuscripts, but they often produce outputs with unsupported claims and inconsistencies. Traditional systems organize research as sequential pipelines without explicitly maintaining the evolving claim-evidence structure. EviGraph addresses this by using a typed evidence graph as the operational state, allowing the agent to validate and repair evidence chains across stages. This approach is similar to knowledge graphs but focuses on the research process itself.

This study demonstrates that gradient-based attribution methods from large language models (LLMs) can predict fMRI brain activity, and in early auditory regions they outperform the models' internal representations. The researchers also extended attribution to individual layers using conductance, revealing distinct computational roles across layers. This work bridges explainable AI and neuroscience, offering new insights into why LLM representations align with brain activity. It could influence future research in interpretability and cognitive modeling, potentially leading to more brain-aligned AI systems. The study used gradient-based attribution methods, such as integrated gradients, to quantify each input word's contribution to next-word predictions, and then used these explanations to predict fMRI data from participants listening to narratives. Conductance was used to extend attribution from words to individual layers, showing that early layers exhibit word-type sensitivity and align with auditory regions, while the final layer's attribution is dominated by positional information and shows broad cortical alignment.

## [CRINN: RL-Driven Automated ANNS Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02091) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CRINN introduces a novel paradigm that frames approximate nearest-neighbor search (ANNS) optimization as a reinforcement learning problem, using execution speed as the reward signal to automatically generate faster implementations. It achieves state-of-the-art results on three of six benchmark datasets and ties for first on two others. This work demonstrates that reinforcement learning-augmented LLMs can automate sophisticated algorithmic optimizations, potentially reducing the need for manual, expert-driven tuning in critical AI infrastructure like vector search. It could accelerate the development of faster ANNS algorithms, benefiting RAG and agent-based LLM applications that rely on efficient retrieval. CRINN uses contrastive reinforcement learning to generate thousands of code variations, test them, and learn what makes vector search fast while maintaining accuracy constraints. The method was evaluated on six widely-used NNS benchmark datasets, including GIST-960-Euclidean, MNIST-784-Euclidean, and GloVe-25-angular, and the code is open-sourced on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Approximate nearest neighbor search (ANNS) is a family of algorithms that trade a small amount of accuracy for significant speedups in finding similar items in high-dimensional spaces. It is a critical component in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and vector databases, where fast retrieval is essential. Traditional ANNS optimization relies on manual, expert-driven tuning of data structures and parameters, which is labor-intensive and requires specialized knowledge.

## [Scaling Laws for Shallow Neural Networks in Feature Learning Regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24882) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper derives scaling laws for quadratic and diagonal neural networks in the feature learning regime, linking them to matrix compressed sensing and LASSO, and provides a phase diagram of scaling exponents and spectral properties. This work extends theoretical understanding of neural scaling laws beyond linear models, offering a first-principles validation of empirical observations about power-law tails in weight spectra and generalization. It could inform the design and interpretation of deep learning models. The analysis reveals crossovers between distinct scaling regimes and plateau behaviors, and establishes a precise link between these regimes and the spectral properties of trained network weights. The phase diagram maps scaling exponents as functions of sample complexity and weight decay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Neural scaling laws describe how model performance improves with data, parameters, or compute, but theoretical understanding has been mostly limited to linear models. The feature learning regime is where neural networks adapt their hidden representations during training, contrasting with the lazy (NTK) regime where weights barely move. Matrix compressed sensing and LASSO are techniques for recovering low-rank or sparse signals from limited measurements, providing a mathematical connection to neural network training.

## [Pretrained Model Leak Enables Jailbreak Transfer to Finetuned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14751) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that adversarial prompts optimized on a pretrained large language model can transfer to its finetuned derivatives, revealing inherited jailbreak vulnerabilities. It also proposes the Probe-Guided Projection (PGP) attack and a lightweight defense. This finding highlights a critical security risk in the pretrain-to-finetune paradigm, where attackers can exploit publicly released pretrained models to compromise proprietary finetuned systems. It underscores the need for robust security measures in the LLM supply chain. The PGP attack leverages representation-level probing to identify transferability-relevant directions in hidden states, achieving strong transfer success across multiple LLM families and tasks. The proposed defense mitigates jailbreak transfer while preserving downstream utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Finetuning pretrained LLMs is a standard practice, but its security implications are not fully understood. Jailbreak attacks craft adversarial prompts to bypass safety barriers, and transferability means prompts optimized on one model can work on others. Representation-level probing involves training simple classifiers on hidden states to analyze internal encoding.

## [ABC-GRPO: Bounded Clipping Fixes GRPO's Unbounded Blind Spot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03895) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ABC-GRPO, a modification to GRPO that applies bounded clipping in all four quadrants of the likelihood-ratio/advantage space, specifically addressing the unbounded suppression issue in Q4. The method clips the likelihood ratio before multiplying by the advantage, adding a trust-region floor in Q2 and a cap in Q4. GRPO is a widely used RL algorithm for LLM training, and its unbounded Q4 updates can cause entropy collapse and narrow reasoning boundaries, harming training stability and generalization. ABC-GRPO provides a targeted fix that improves stability and generalization, which is significant for the RLHF and LLM community. ABC-GRPO achieves the highest Avg@64 and Pass@64 on mathematical reasoning with Qwen3 base models, statistically superior to GRPO, SAPO, and dual-clip PPO, and competitive with DAPO, while maintaining higher entropy. Ablations isolate Q4 as the dominant blind spot, and gains transfer to MATH-500 and out-of-domain code (HumanEval).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm for LLMs that inherits PPO's token-level clipping but uses sequence-level advantages. PPO's clipping mechanism limits policy updates to ensure stability, but GRPO's combination leaves one quadrant (Q4) unbounded, allowing excessive suppressive updates. This paper proposes a fix by applying bounded clipping in all quadrants.

<a id="item-113"></a>
## [d3LLM: Ultra-Fast Diffusion LLM via Pseudo-Trajectory Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07568) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces d3LLM, a diffusion large language model that uses pseudo-trajectory distillation during training and entropy-based multi-block decoding with a KV-cache refresh mechanism during inference to balance accuracy and parallelism. It also proposes a new evaluation metric, Accuracy Under Parallelism (AUP), to jointly measure accuracy and parallelism. This work addresses a critical trade-off in diffusion LLMs, which have potential for parallel decoding but often suffer from accuracy loss. By achieving up to 10x speedup over vanilla LLaDA/Dream and 5x over autoregressive models with minimal accuracy drop, d3LLM could make diffusion LLMs more practical for efficient inference, impacting the broader LLM deployment landscape. The pseudo-trajectory distillation teaches the model which tokens can be confidently decoded early, improving parallelism. The entropy-based multi-block decoding adaptively determines block sizes based on token entropy, and the KV-cache refresh mechanism helps maintain accuracy. The AUP metric is hardware-robust and scores the accuracy-parallelism trade-off in a single number.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence, allowing parallel decoding and random-order generation, unlike autoregressive (AR) models that generate token-by-token sequentially. However, dLLMs face an accuracy-parallelism trade-off: more parallel steps can reduce accuracy. Distillation is a technique where a smaller or faster model learns from a larger teacher model, and pseudo-trajectory distillation uses the teacher's intermediate states to guide the student. Entropy-based decoding uses the model's uncertainty to decide how many tokens to decode at once.

## [PhaseCoder: Geometry-Agnostic Spatial Audio for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21124) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhaseCoder introduces a transformer-only spatial audio encoder that is agnostic to microphone geometry, taking raw multichannel audio and microphone coordinates as inputs to produce robust spatial embeddings. It enables Gemma 3n LLM to reason over 'Spatial Audio Tokens' and achieve state-of-the-art results on microphone-invariant localization benchmarks, enabling complex spatial reasoning and targeted transcription from arbitrary microphone arrays for the first time. This work bridges a critical gap in multimodal LLMs by incorporating spatial audio information, which is essential for embodied AI applications such as robotics and augmented reality. By making the model geometry-agnostic, it enables deployment across diverse devices with varying microphone configurations, potentially accelerating the adoption of spatial audio understanding in real-world systems. PhaseCoder is a transformer-only encoder that processes raw multichannel audio and microphone coordinates to perform localization and generate spatial embeddings. The paper demonstrates fine-tuning Gemma 3n LLM to reason over these spatial audio tokens, achieving state-of-the-art results on microphone-invariant localization benchmarks and enabling targeted transcription from arbitrary microphone arrays.

## [Layer Pruning Severely Impairs LLM Generative Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01997) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that layer pruning of large language models (LLMs) severely impairs generative reasoning capabilities, with limited recovery even after supervised finetuning, unlike classification tasks. The study evaluates a simple recovery strategy using a single 80GB GPU and finds that while classification tasks recover up to 90% of baseline performance, generative reasoning tasks like GSM8K and HumanEval+ show substantially weaker recovery. This finding challenges the generalizability of layer pruning as a model compression technique, highlighting important trade-offs for efficiency research. It provides practical guidance for when depth reduction is effective under constrained post-training regimes, which is crucial for deploying LLMs in resource-limited environments. The paper shows that pruning leads to loss of key algorithmic capabilities, including arithmetic computation and balanced parenthesis generation, beyond surface-level text degradation. Even under a favorable task-aligned recovery setting, where pruned models are fully finetuned on self-generated GSM8K responses, the gap persists, and deficits remain even in a depth-pruned model trained with nearly 100B post-pruning tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Layer pruning is a model compression technique that removes entire layers from a neural network to reduce its size and computational cost. Previous work suggested that LLMs can be pruned with minimal performance loss on classification benchmarks, but this paper reveals that generative reasoning tasks, which require multi-step logic and arithmetic, are more sensitive to such structural changes. The study uses benchmarks like GSM8K (grade school math problems) and HumanEval+ (code generation tasks) to evaluate reasoning capabilities.

## [MARS: Adaptive Data Augmentation for Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17658) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MARS introduces an adaptive augmentation framework for reward modeling that allocates more augmentation to low-margin preference pairs and uses semantic-distance-based refinement to improve chosen-rejected contrast before generating synthetic preference samples. It consistently improves RewardBench performance and alignment win rates across three preference datasets and two reward-model backbones. This addresses a critical bottleneck in RLHF and RLAIF: the scarcity and heterogeneity of human preference data, which limits reward model reliability. By improving reward modeling with a simple yet effective augmentation strategy, MARS could enhance alignment outcomes for LLMs, benefiting the broader AI alignment community. MARS outperforms uniform augmentation, WoN, and AdaBoost-style baselines on average RewardBench performance and alignment win rates. Ablations and independent-judge evaluations suggest the gains are not solely explained by semantic refinement alone or GPT-4.1 judge coupling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Reward modeling is a core component of RLHF and RLAIF, where a model learns to predict human preferences. However, reward models often struggle to generalize due to limited and diverse preference data. Data augmentation is a common technique to address data scarcity, but traditional methods may not effectively target the most informative samples. MARS leverages the concept of margin (the difference in scores between chosen and rejected responses) to focus augmentation on ambiguous pairs, and uses semantic distance to refine the contrast.

## [OnlineSPEC: Evolving Draft Models via Speculative Decoding Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12617) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OnlineSPEC introduces a unified framework that treats speculative decoding as an online learning problem, using verification feedback to continuously adapt draft models. It achieves up to 24% speedup over seven benchmarks and five foundation models. This work addresses a key limitation in speculative decoding—draft models often fail to match the target distribution, limiting speedup. By enabling draft models to evolve from feedback, it could significantly improve inference efficiency for LLMs, benefiting real-time applications and reducing computational costs. The framework is grounded in dynamic regret minimization, establishing a formal link between online learning performance and acceleration rate. It includes optimistic online learning that reuses historical gradients and online ensemble learning that maintains multiple draft models, all with theoretical justifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a small draft model to generate candidate tokens, which a larger target model verifies in parallel. This process preserves output distribution but often suffers from draft-target mismatch. Online learning is a machine learning paradigm where models update continuously from streaming data, contrasting with batch learning.

## [ORCA: Test-Time Training Calibrates LLM Reasoning with Conformal Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ORCA introduces a meta-learning framework that calibrates LLM sampling during test time using conformal prediction, providing valid confidence estimates under distributional shift and improving efficiency. At risk level δ=0.1, it achieves up to 47.5% savings on in-distribution tasks for Qwen2.5-32B and boosts MATH-500 savings from 24.8% to 67.0% in zero-shot out-of-domain settings. This addresses the critical issue of miscalibration in test-time scaling, which causes exorbitant compute costs for state-of-the-art LLM reasoning. By providing theoretical guarantees and empirical gains, ORCA could make LLM inference more efficient and reliable, benefiting researchers and practitioners deploying LLMs in dynamic environments. ORCA updates the calibration module for each input via a meta-learning procedure, enabling adaptation to thought patterns across reasoning stages and prompt distribution shifts. The framework provides theoretical guarantees on conformal risks and demonstrates improvements across model families and downstream benchmarks, with code publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling improves LLM performance on difficult tasks but at high compute cost due to miscalibration. Conformal prediction is a framework for quantifying uncertainty by providing prediction sets with guaranteed coverage, and test-time training adapts model parameters during inference. ORCA combines these ideas to calibrate the sampling process, offering a principled way to improve efficiency and generalization.

The paper introduces Ge2mS-T, a novel spiking vision transformer architecture that implements grouped computation across temporal, spatial, and network structure dimensions. It proposes the Grouped-Exponential-Coding-based IF (ExpG-IF) model for lossless conversion and Group-wise Spiking Self-Attention (GW-SSA) to reduce computational complexity. This work addresses the critical trade-off between memory overhead, learning capability, and energy budget in spiking vision transformers, potentially enabling more energy-efficient AI for edge devices. It is the first to systematically establish multi-dimensional grouped computation for S-ViTs, which could influence future SNN research and applications. The ExpG-IF model enables lossless ANN-to-SNN conversion with constant training overhead and precise spike pattern regulation. GW-SSA uses multi-scale token grouping and multiplication-free operations within a hybrid attention-convolution framework, reducing computational complexity. The code is available on GitHub.

Vision transformers (ViTs) have become popular in computer vision but are computationally heavy, making them difficult to deploy on energy-constrained devices. This paper aims to combine the benefits of SNNs and ViTs by introducing grouped computation to improve efficiency.

## [LLM Mental Health Simulations Fail Population-Level Fidelity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17359) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new audit tested four large language models (GPT-4o-mini, Gemini-3-Flash, DeepSeek-V3, GLM-4.7) on simulating psychiatric patients across 120 demographic cohorts, scoring 28,800 responses against PHQ-8 anchors from NHANES data. While individual cases passed DSM-5 gateway rules 97.3% of the time, simulated populations systematically overestimated depression severity and failed to reproduce real-world demographic disparities. This research highlights a critical gap between case-level plausibility and population-level validity in LLM-based mental health simulations, which could mislead clinical training, research, and policy if used without caution. The findings underscore the need for population-level auditing before deploying LLMs in mental health applications. The study found that every benchmarkable group was inflated by 2.8 to 5.5 PHQ-8 points, and 18.2% of simulated patients screened at the treatment threshold versus 7.5% of real adults. Additionally, symptom covariance reorganized by cohort, demographic offsets did not stack, and a third of patients changed severity category between two draws of the same prompt, with one in five crossing the treatment line.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: PHQ-8 is a validated eight-item self-report scale used to screen for depression severity over the past two weeks. NHANES is a national survey that combines interviews and physical examinations to assess health and nutritional status, providing representative population data. The study used survey-weighted PHQ-8 anchors derived from NHANES microdata to evaluate the simulations.

<a id="item-123"></a>
## [Supervised Learning's Geometric Blind Spot and Isotropic Encoder Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21395) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies a geometric blind spot in supervised learning where representation drift along nuisance directions is unpenalized, and proposes isotropic encoder matching to mitigate it. Experiments on CIFAR-10 with a Vision Transformer show that isotropic matching achieves the best clean layout score (0.904) compared to task-only training (1.093) and adversarial training (1.353). This work challenges the conventional focus on task loss and Jacobian magnitude in supervised learning, revealing a fundamental limitation that affects robustness and generalization. It provides a theoretical foundation and practical guidance for designing encoders that are less sensitive to nuisance variations, potentially improving adversarial robustness and out-of-distribution performance. The paper proves a drift floor for linear-Gaussian models and uses a class-layout score to track the dissociation between Jacobian magnitude and clean class geometry. The design rule suggests reporting class-layout geometry alongside task scores and preferring isotropic encoder matching when nuisance axes are unknown.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Supervised learning typically minimizes a task loss without considering how representations change along directions that are irrelevant for the task but may be harmful at deployment. This 'geometric blind spot' can lead to non-robust features, texture bias, and corruption fragility. The paper proposes isotropic encoder matching, which penalizes the squared distance between representations of inputs perturbed by Gaussian noise, to reduce sensitivity to nuisance directions.

Some emphasize the need to report class-layout geometry alongside task scores.

## [Dream-MPC: Gradient-Based MPC with Latent Imagination for Efficient Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04568) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Dream-MPC introduces a novel hybrid model-based RL approach that combines gradient-based optimization with latent imagination and policy priors, generating candidate trajectories and optimizing them via gradient ascent. It significantly improves policy performance across 24 continuous control tasks, outperforming gradient-free MPC and state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses a known limitation in model-based RL where gradient-based planning often underperforms gradient-free methods. By demonstrating that gradient-based MPC can be made efficient and effective, Dream-MPC could enable faster and more scalable planning for high-dimensional control tasks, benefiting robotics and autonomous systems. Dream-MPC uses a learned world model, uncertainty regularization, and amortization of optimization iterations by reusing previously optimized actions. The approach generates few candidate trajectories from a rolled-out policy and optimizes each via gradient ascent, with code and videos available at the project website.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Model-based reinforcement learning (RL) aims to improve sample efficiency by learning a dynamics model and using it for planning or generating experience. Model Predictive Control (MPC) is a planning method that optimizes actions over a finite horizon, often using gradient-free population-based methods. Hybrid approaches combine MPC with a learned policy prior to leverage both planning and policy learning, but they typically rely on computationally expensive gradient-free optimization. Gradient-based methods are a promising alternative but have historically underperformed, which Dream-MPC aims to overcome.

## [Skill Neologisms: Soft Tokens for Scalable Continual Learning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'skill neologisms'—soft tokens added to a model's vocabulary—that enable selective skill acquisition without weight updates. Experiments on synthetic tasks and the Skill-Mix benchmark demonstrate that these tokens can be learned and composed zero-shot. This approach offers a scalable path to continual learning in LLMs, addressing catastrophic forgetting and context limitations. It could enable models to acquire new skills dynamically without expensive retraining, benefiting applications requiring adaptable AI systems. The method involves optimizing soft tokens integrated into the vocabulary, with no weight updates. The paper shows that pretrained LLMs already have tokens associated with procedural knowledge, and independently trained skill neologisms can be composed zero-shot on the Skill-Mix benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning in LLMs is challenging due to catastrophic forgetting and the high cost of retraining. Traditional fine-tuning risks forgetting previous skills, while context-based methods are limited by context length. Soft prompt tuning, a related technique, adds trainable tokens to the input, but skill neologisms extend this by integrating tokens into the vocabulary for skill-specific acquisition.

## [Verifiable Process Supervision Improves LLM Reasoning Accuracy and Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12519) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces verifiable process supervision (VPS), a post-training framework that supervises intermediate claims to jointly optimize prediction accuracy and reasoning quality in language models. VPS uses adaptive weighting to prioritize reasoning components with the largest remaining errors, creating an implicit curriculum. This work addresses a key limitation of reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), which often improves accuracy at the expense of reasoning quality. By preserving both accuracy and reasoning soundness, VPS could enhance the reliability of LLMs in verifiable domains like math and chess, impacting AI safety and reasoning research. In chess experiments, outcome-only RL increased win-rate error by up to 112% and reduced internal consistency by up to 69%, while VPS reduced win-rate error by up to 30% and restored consistency to near saturation. The framework also showed similar benefits in math reasoning, where outcome-only RL degraded step-level arithmetic and consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a common post-training method that uses outcome-based rewards to improve LLM accuracy. However, it often neglects the quality of intermediate reasoning steps. Process supervision, which assigns rewards to each reasoning step, has been explored but is often expensive due to human annotation. VPS automates this by using structured intermediate claims that can be deterministically verified, as in chess with engine signals.

## [Fast Rates Proven for Inverse Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14599) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that entropy-regularized min-max inverse reinforcement learning (Min-Max-IRL) achieves fast statistical rates, with excess trajectory-level KL divergence and squared parameter error decaying at O(n^{-1}) for linear reward classes. It also establishes equivalence between MLE and Min-Max-IRL at the population level and under deterministic dynamics. These results provide the first fast-rate guarantees for Min-Max-IRL, bridging a gap between theory and practice and potentially influencing future IRL algorithm design. The equivalence with MLE also unifies two major IRL paradigms, which could simplify theoretical analysis and guide practical implementations. The guarantees hold under misspecification and do not require uniform state-coverage assumptions. A local minimax lower bound matches the parameter-error rate up to logarithmic factors in the well-specified deterministic setting, and reward-identifiability results are extended to general Borel spaces.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Inverse reinforcement learning (IRL) aims to recover the reward function of an expert from observed behavior. Min-Max-IRL formulates this as a minimax optimization problem, often with entropy regularization to improve identifiability. Statistical rates describe how quickly estimation error decreases with more data; fast rates like O(n^{-1}) are desirable. Pseudo-self-concordance is a property of loss functions that enables such fast-rate analysis.

## [CP-MoE: Consistency-Preserving MoE for Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20247) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CP-MoE introduces a transient expert and a consistency-preserving routing bias to mitigate catastrophic forgetting in continual learning for LLMs and VLMs. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on SuperNI and VQA v2 benchmarks. This work addresses a critical trade-off in MoE-based continual learning, balancing knowledge transfer and forgetting prevention. It could enable more efficient and scalable continual learning for large models, impacting both research and practical deployment. The framework uses a transient expert to capture early task-specific updates and guide integration into stable experts. It also employs a transient expert-guided regularization mechanism to protect important historical parameters during merging, validated on both unimodal and multimodal benchmarks.

## [Domain-Gated Latent Diffusion Designs Novel Energetic Materials with DFT Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26540) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Domain-Gated Latent Diffusion (DGLD), a generative model that explicitly incorporates data reliability into the training process by sorting labels into four trust tiers. DGLD proposes 10 novel molecules unknown to PubChem, with the best candidate, 3,4,5-trinitro-1,2-isoxazole, matching the performance of benchmark explosives HMX and PETN in DFT calculations. This work addresses a critical data quality issue in generative materials design, where most training data are unreliable. By demonstrating that trust gating can improve the validity of generated molecules, DGLD could influence broader generative modeling practices beyond chemistry, especially in fields with abundant weak data surrounding a reliable core. DGLD uses a four-stage chemistry-validation funnel ending in a quantum-chemical DFT audit, and the best molecule has a four-step synthesis route. The trust gating mechanism is chemistry-independent, making it applicable to other domains with similar data reliability issues.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Energetic materials are used in mining, demolition, propulsion, and airbags, but most current compounds were designed decades ago. Generative models are natural tools for exploring the vast molecular space, but their training data often contain unreliable estimates; only about 3,000 of 66,000 molecules with recorded properties were measured or computed from first principles. First-principles calculations, such as density functional theory (DFT), provide accurate predictions without empirical parameters, serving as a reliable validation method.
