## [BenHalluEval: New Benchmark for Bengali LLM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31483) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BenHalluEval introduces a multi-task hallucination evaluation framework for Bengali, covering four tasks and 12,000 hallucinated candidates generated by GPT-5.4. It also proposes BenHalluScore, a dual-track calibration metric that ranges from 7.72% to 55.42% across models and tasks. This is the first dedicated hallucination benchmark for Bengali, a major language with over 230 million speakers, addressing a significant gap in multilingual NLP evaluation. The proposed dual-track metric highlights the inadequacy of single-track evaluation approaches for low-resource languages, potentially influencing future evaluation practices. The framework evaluates seven LLMs across reasoning-oriented, multilingual, and Bengali-centric categories under a dual-track protocol: Track A measures false-positive rate on ground-truth instances, and Track B measures hallucination detection rate on hallucinated candidates. Chain-of-thought prompting was tested as a mitigation strategy but did not consistently improve hallucination discrimination.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Hallucination in large language models refers to the generation of plausible but incorrect or unfounded content. Evaluating hallucination is crucial for ensuring reliability, especially for low-resource languages like Bengali, which have been underrepresented in NLP research. Existing benchmarks often focus on high-resource languages and use single-track metrics, which may not capture the full picture of model performance.

## [PrivacyPeek: Auditing LLM Agents' Data Acquisition Privacy Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00152) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PrivacyPeek introduces a benchmark with 1,182 cases across 7 acquisition behaviors and 16 application domains to audit privacy leakage at the data acquisition stage of LLM-based agents. It uses Acquisition Inspection to detect over-acquisition and Probe Elicitation to measure how easily attackers can elicit acquired sensitive information. This benchmark addresses a critical blind spot in existing privacy evaluations, which focus on response disclosure but ignore the acquisition stage where data first enters the agent's context. By highlighting widespread over-acquisition and its correlation with task capability, it urges the community to prioritize acquisition-stage privacy auditing in agent design and security. Experiments on 10 LLM-based agents across 4 model families show that unnecessary acquisition of sensitive information is widespread, and prompt-level defenses reduce only a small fraction of leakage. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/Xuan269/PrivacyPeek-Resource.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based agents autonomously invoke external tools to complete multi-step tasks, often acquiring more sensitive data than needed. Existing privacy benchmarks typically audit what the agent discloses in responses or outgoing actions, but overlook the acquisition stage where data first enters the agent's context. This over-acquired information can be leaked through careless actions or attacks, making acquisition-stage auditing essential.

## [Mean-Expansion Layer Accelerates Q-Learning by Sharing Action Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29806) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the mean-expansion layer, a parameter-free addition to Q-network architectures that accelerates action-value learning by sharing values across actions within a state. Applied to deep Q-networks and implicit quantile networks, it improves aggregate performance across 57 Atari 2600 games. This work addresses a fundamental inefficiency in Q-learning, where action values are learned independently and slowly from near-zero initialization. The mean-expansion layer has the potential for broad impact on deep RL architectures, improving learning speed and reducing value overestimation. The mean-expansion layer changes the learning target from potentially large action values to a lower-norm representation, which accelerates learning. It is parameter-free and can be added to existing Q-networks without altering the underlying algorithm, and it also increases action gaps and dramatically reduces value overestimation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Q-learning is a foundational reinforcement learning algorithm where an agent learns action values, which represent the expected return of taking an action in a given state. Traditional Q-learning updates each state-action pair independently, which can be slow when true values are far from initialization. The mean-expansion layer addresses this by sharing values across actions within a state, reducing the norm of the learned representation.

The paper introduces CostAda, a cost-calibrated adaptive controller that incorporates token costs into credit assignment for LLM-driven discovery. It achieves the strongest baseline's full-budget quality with at most half the budget on twelve of sixteen benchmark-backbone pairs. This addresses a critical gap in LLM-based discovery by making search cost-aware, which is essential for practical deployment under token budgets. It could significantly improve efficiency and scalability of LLM-driven scientific and algorithmic discovery. CostAda uses cost-calibrated frontier utility, which values frontier progress relative to realized action cost and conditions on remaining budget. It controls local exploration intensity, frontier allocation, and budgeted tactic intervention, with theoretical guarantees showing cost-blind credit can forfeit all but a vanishing fraction of attainable quality.

CostAda addresses this by integrating cost into the credit assignment, making the search process more budget-aware.

## [WitCert: Sound Runtime Meter for KV-Cache Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28699) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WitCert introduces a provably sound runtime meter for KV-cache quantization, providing per-layer, per-head, and per-step upper bounds on total variation between exact and compressed attention. It is implemented in SGLang and includes machine-checked proofs in Lean 4. This addresses a critical gap in deployed LLM serving systems, where quantization errors are only validated offline. It enables real-time detection and gating of compression-induced errors, improving reliability and trustworthiness of LLM inference at scale. The meter has two tiers: a deterministic band-norm-witness bound for any cache-preserving black-box quantizer, and a probabilistic certificate for a subtractively-dithered INT8 quantizer. In experiments, gating restored quality from 22.8 to 79.7 on hard RULER tasks, and the certified int8 cache served 1.88x more KV tokens at the same memory.

**Background**: KV-cache quantization reduces memory usage in LLM serving by compressing the key-value cache, but it can introduce errors that degrade output quality. Traditional validation relies on offline benchmarks, which do not reflect real-time conditions. WitCert provides a runtime meter to monitor and gate quantization errors during live serving.

This paper introduces an online embodiment adaptation framework for quadrupedal locomotion that infers embodiment parameters from short interaction histories and conditions control on the inferred hardware state. The method enables a Unitree Go2 robot to adapt to joint-range constraints and trunk-mass changes within half a second, outperforming history-conditioned policies. This work addresses a critical challenge in legged robotics: policy robustness to hardware changes. By enabling rapid adaptation to physical changes, it improves the practicality and reliability of quadrupedal robots in real-world applications, potentially impacting embodied AI and robotics industries. The framework pairs a generalist policy trained under embodiment randomization with a lightweight adaptation module that identifies physical changes within half a second. In simulation, it accurately estimates joint-range and mass changes, and on a real Unitree Go2 robot, it maintains stable locomotion under severe changes like a fully locked leg and a 5 kg payload, where non-adaptive methods fail.

Embodiment randomization is a technique that trains policies under varied physical parameters to improve robustness, but it may not handle unseen changes. This work proposes explicit online embodiment identification to adapt to changes in real-time, which is a step toward handling broader forms of uncertain or degraded hardware.

<a id="item-137"></a>
## [OpenAI Privacy Filter Evaluated Across 32 Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02616) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first systematic evaluation of OpenAI's Privacy Filter (OPF), a 1.5B-parameter PII detection model, across 32 benchmarks spanning 14 languages and 5 domains. The study reveals domain-dependent data efficiency trade-offs and significant performance degradation outside its training distribution. This evaluation provides critical insights for practitioners choosing between OPF and fine-tuned models like XLM-RoBERTa, highlighting when zero-shot OPF is sufficient and when fine-tuning is more data-efficient. The findings have direct implications for privacy tool deployment in multilingual and domain-specific contexts. Fine-tuned XLM-RoBERTa surpasses OPF's zero-shot performance with only ~500 labeled examples on English synthetic PII, ~100 on non-English Kiji, and ~1000 on synthetic medical PII. OPF achieves F1=0.464 on SPY medical benchmark and F1=0.855 on AI4Privacy, but collapses on general NER and non-Latin scripts (Arabic: 0.04, Cyrillic: 0.03).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: OpenAI Privacy Filter is an open-weight bidirectional token-classification model for PII detection and masking, pretrained autoregressively with architecture similar to gpt-oss but smaller. PII detection is crucial for privacy protection, and models like XLM-RoBERTa are commonly fine-tuned for such tasks. This evaluation helps understand OPF's strengths and limitations across languages and domains.

<a id="item-138"></a>
## [Output-Aware Rotation for INT2 KV-Cache Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02691) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OptR, an output-aware rotation method for INT2 KV-cache quantization that minimizes post-W_O attention-output error. It decomposes the error into key- and value-induced terms and learns per-head orthogonal corrections through the full quantization and attention path. KV cache is a major bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, and INT2 quantization can significantly reduce memory usage. OptR improves upon existing methods like QuaRot and OSCAR, enhancing long-context retrieval and overall inference efficiency, which is crucial for deploying LLMs with longer contexts. OptR applies an attention-equivalent key reparameterization to reduce large channel-wise offsets without changing the softmax distribution. It preserves the paged KV-cache format and adds negligible inference overhead, as validated across three models and five benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache quantization reduces memory footprint by storing key and value tensors in lower precision, such as INT2. Rotation-based methods apply orthogonal transformations to spread outliers across dimensions, making quantization easier. However, existing methods optimize proxy errors before the full attention readout, while OptR directly minimizes the final attention output error after the output projection.

## [EuroExec Benchmark Shows Frontier LLMs Underperform Experts on Executive Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04549) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new benchmark, EuroExec, comprising 413 open-ended European executive tasks authored by 47 domain experts, was introduced. After over 4,000 human expert hours of evaluation, the best frontier LLM achieved only a 56.9% solve rate, while expert-written reference answers were preferred over every model response in 74% of direct rankings. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLMs on open-ended, real-world problems with subjective ground truth, showing that frontier models fall well below professional standards. The findings have significant implications for AI deployment in high-stakes executive decision-making roles, highlighting the need for human oversight. The evaluation used a multi-attribute rubric, item-specific checklists, and preference ranking to compute a 'Solve Rate' metric. The study also found that automatic measurements fall short when evaluating such open-ended problems, emphasizing the importance of human evaluators and rigorous statistical consistency checks.

## [MSRT Breaks Curse of Multilinguality in Speech Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04586) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MSRT, a framework with a resource-aware Mixture of Speech Encoders (MoSE) and a five-stage curriculum learning strategy, achieving state-of-the-art performance on 45 languages in many-to-many speech-to-text translation. This work addresses the curse of multilinguality, where high-resource languages dominate and low-resource languages suffer. By improving low-resource performance without compromising high-resource languages, it could enable more inclusive and effective multilingual speech translation systems. The 4B-parameter model outperforms substantially larger baselines, requiring only 10 hours of paired S2TT data per language. The MoSE uses an explicit language router to assign utterances to a frozen expert for high-resource languages and a trainable expert for medium- and low-resource languages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: The curse of multilinguality refers to the performance degradation in multilingual models due to competition for limited representation capacity among languages. Speech-to-text translation (S2TT) aims to translate speech from a source language into text in a target language. Mixture of experts (MoE) architectures use multiple specialized sub-models to handle different inputs, and curriculum learning gradually increases task difficulty during training.

## [RepoProbe: A New Benchmark for LLM Repository Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04783) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RepoProbe introduces a novel benchmark for evaluating LLM repository-level code understanding using open-ended Q&A from GitHub Discussions, paired with a Checklist-Based Verification Protocol that decomposes answers into atomic, verifiable facts to replace subjective scalar scoring. This benchmark addresses the critical issue of Edit Bias in LLM code generation, where models prematurely propose code modifications instead of understanding existing architecture, and improves evaluation reliability by reducing variance and enhancing interpretability, which is significant for advancing AI-assisted software engineering. The evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs reveals a persistent gap between high clarity and evidence-grounded technical correctness, and quantitatively confirms the prevalence of edit bias. The verification protocol significantly improves evaluation reliability compared to traditional scalar scoring methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Existing benchmarks for repository-level code understanding often rely on bug reports from GitHub Issues, which allow models to bypass genuine understanding via pattern matching on error logs. LLM-as-a-Judge scalar scoring suffers from high variance and low interpretability. RepoProbe uses GitHub Discussions to focus on open-ended architectural inquiries, and its checklist-based verification protocol decomposes answers into atomic facts for objective verification.

## [SkillTrace: Multi-Trace Provenance Auditing for LLM-Agent Skill Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05204) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillTrace is a new framework that audits LLM-agent skill reuse by extracting three provenance traces (Expression, Implementation, and Operational) and representing the Operational Trace as a Skill Operational Graph (SOG). It achieves AUROC 0.938 and F1 0.898 on the SKILLTRACE-BENCH benchmark with 820 transformed reuse positives and 751 negative controls. This work addresses a novel and timely problem in the rapidly growing LLM-agent ecosystem, where skills are becoming marketplace artifacts and auditing their reuse is critical for quality control and security. The multi-trace approach could improve how skill reuse is detected and managed, potentially impacting software engineering practices and agent governance. SkillTrace uses an LLM only for Operational-trace extraction at ingestion time, while audit-time comparisons are deterministic and calibrated against same-function strict negatives. A wild audit of 36,446 skills showed that trace-attributed evidence surfaces actionable reuse review queues beyond repository-level baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-agent ecosystems rely on reusable skills, which are mixed-modality packages containing metadata, instructions, code, tools, and workflows. Existing clone detection methods focus on single-modality source code or whole-package similarity, but skill reuse evidence can be distributed across different parts, making it hard to detect. Provenance auditing tracks the origin and history of data or artifacts, and SkillTrace applies this concept to skill reuse by analyzing multiple traces.

## [Abstract Event Causal Rules: A New Paradigm for Generalizable Causal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05205) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Abstract Event Causal Rules (AECR), a relation-level causal abstraction paradigm that converts concrete cause-effect pairs into generalized abstract causal logic, and proposes a multi-agent Concrete-to-Abstract Causal Induction (CACI) system to distill trustworthy AECRs from noisy data, building two complete AECR knowledge bases. This work addresses the long-tail generalization problem in event-centric AI, where instance-level causal pairs fail on rare or unseen event combinations. By providing abstract causal knowledge, it enhances the generalization capacity of event causal reasoning and improves event prediction, which is crucial for applications like risk early warning and decision support. The proposed Abstract Rule-Guided Causal Attention Encoder (AR-GCAE) injects retrieved AECRs into the causality Graph Event Prediction (CGEP) benchmark via rule-guided attention layers and gated representation fusion. Experimental results show consistent performance improvements, especially on rare and unseen event samples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Event-centric intelligent systems rely on explicit causal event knowledge for tasks like risk early warning and narrative comprehension. However, instance-level causal pairs often suffer from poor generalization on low-frequency and unseen event combinations. AECR aims to abstract these pairs into general rules, and the multi-agent CACI system uses similarity-constrained clustering to handle noisy raw data, building knowledge bases that can be applied to downstream tasks.

PD-GS introduces a phoneme-driven 3D Gaussian Splatting method with a Linguistic Fusion Module (LFM) that fuses continuous audio context with discrete phoneme embeddings to improve lip articulation and reduce the 'leaky mouth' artifact. On the HDTF dataset, it achieves the best lip geometry (LMD 2.66) among compared baselines. This work addresses a persistent challenge in audio-driven talking head synthesis—accurate lip articulation—by leveraging explicit phoneme alignment, which could lead to more realistic and linguistically faithful neural avatars. It offers a practical improvement over existing methods that rely solely on continuous audio embeddings, potentially benefiting applications in virtual reality, film production, and human-computer interaction. PD-GS is trained purely from monocular video using image reconstruction and lip landmark supervision. The Linguistic Fusion Module uses a learned gate to adaptively fuse audio and phoneme features, strengthening phoneme guidance on articulation-critical segments while preserving smooth audio-driven dynamics.

However, audio-driven methods often suffer from over-smoothed mouth motion and violations of articulatory constraints, such as bilabial closures, leading to the 'leaky mouth' artifact. Phonemes are discrete linguistic units that correspond to specific articulatory gestures, and forced alignment can provide time-aligned phoneme tokens from audio, which can help disambiguate closure-level events.

## [State-Matched Routing and Contextualized Self-Distillation for Multi-Turn Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05219) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SMRC-SD, a method that mitigates state-reference mismatch in privileged on-policy distillation for multi-turn agents. It improves task success rates on ALFWorld from 0.746 to 0.865 and on WebShop from 0.574 to 0.693 using Qwen3-1.7B. This work addresses a critical issue in training multi-turn agents with privileged guidance, which is common in reinforcement learning and LLM agent development. By improving the reliability of distillation, it can lead to more effective and robust agents in interactive environments, benefiting the broader AI agent community. SMRC-SD verifies state matching at each turn and applies distillation only at matched states, constructing state-conditioned teacher context from successful trajectories. The method consistently outperforms unconditional full-path distillation, and ablation studies confirm the contributions of both routing and context construction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Privileged on-policy distillation provides dense supervision by allowing a teacher to re-score the student's responses using training-only references, such as successful trajectories. However, in interactive environments, the student's actions can lead to states not covered by the reference, causing state-reference mismatch. SMRC-SD addresses this by selectively applying distillation at matched states and using state-conditioned context.

## [Project2Task: Graph-Guided Planning for Autonomous Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05225) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Project2Task introduces a graph-based planning layer that decomposes research projects into bounded, dependency-aware tasks using innovation atoms and a directed lineage graph. It selects among horizontal, vertical, and hybrid decompositions via a Bernoulli block-model objective, and generates task contracts with explicit ownership and execution order. This addresses a critical gap in long-horizon project planning for autonomous research agents, enabling coherent, non-redundant, and executable task portfolios. It could significantly improve the efficiency and reliability of AI-driven research automation, impacting fields like scientific discovery and literature-based hypothesis generation. On a benchmark of ten project briefs yielding roughly 30 tasks, Project2Task achieved an average quality score of 7.15, compared to 4.58 for the Brief Baseline and 5.31 for the Topic-only Setting. Integrating its contracts with AutoResearchClaw increased average downstream task accuracy from 0.536 to 0.759.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous research agents can perform tasks like literature search, hypothesis generation, and experiment execution, but they often treat a research project as a single oversized task, leading to vague or overlapping tasks. Project2Task introduces a graph-based representation where candidate contributions are 'innovation atoms' organized in a directed lineage graph, and uses a Bernoulli block-model objective to choose an optimal decomposition. This approach is inspired by concepts like data lineage graphs and stochastic block models, which are used in network analysis and data governance.

## [TriQua Framework Balances Granularity and Context in LLM Factuality Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05228) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TriQua introduces a flexible fact representation that uses standard triples for simple claims and hyperrelational facts with qualifiers for complex claims, along with TriQuaScore for quantifying factuality. Empirical results show TriQuaScore aligns well with human annotations and outperforms existing decomposition-based frameworks in evidence-based fact verification. This work addresses a fundamental trade-off in the decompose-then-verify paradigm for LLM factuality evaluation, potentially improving the accuracy and explainability of fact-checking systems. It could benefit researchers and developers working on LLM evaluation, knowledge verification, and trustworthy AI. TriQua models simple claims as standard triples and complex claims as hyperrelational facts with auxiliary qualifiers, preserving context without sacrificing atomicity. The verification process annotates concrete errors within specific triples and qualifiers, providing fine-grained explainability. TriQuaScore is proposed to quantify the factuality of these structured fact units.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: The decompose-then-verify paradigm for LLM factuality evaluation splits generated text into atomic facts and verifies each against a knowledge source. However, atomic facts often omit essential context, while broader statements lack granularity. Hyperrelational facts, which consist of a main triple and auxiliary qualifiers, have been used in knowledge graph representation to capture more comprehensive information. TriQua leverages this concept to balance granularity and context in factuality evaluation.

## [LUNAR Benchmark Evaluates LLM Personalization from App Logs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05246) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LUNAR is a new benchmark for evaluating LLM personalization using longitudinal app interaction logs across daily-life domains like clothing, food, housing, and mobility. It introduces a multi-stage synthesis pipeline and tests 19 LLMs, revealing that behavioral logs are necessary but not sufficient for deep personalization. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing personalized LLM benchmarks, which often rely on textual personas or isolated signals. It provides insights into how LLMs can leverage real-world behavioral data, impacting the development of more effective and privacy-aware personalization systems. The multi-stage coarse-to-fine synthesis pipeline is grounded in real-world behavioral patterns, showing closer alignment with real distributions than other synthetic benchmarks. Experiments show that direct retrieval of fine-grained behavioral records outperforms compressed memory, and stronger personalization can come at the cost of privacy protection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Personalized LLMs aim to tailor responses to individual users, but existing benchmarks often use simplified personas or isolated signals. LUNAR uses longitudinal app interaction logs to capture heterogeneous daily-life activities, providing a more realistic evaluation. The benchmark also addresses data sparsity and privacy concerns through a synthesis pipeline.

## [Agentic Microscopy Benchmarks Show Limits in Generalizing to Unseen Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05266) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a benchmark and trace-logging framework for evaluating agentic self-driving microscopy systems, testing 105 agent configurations across 53 tasks with 1,949 runs and 49,109 RAG retrievals. It reveals that while benchmarks support qualification and regression testing, surrogate models cannot reliably predict performance on unseen tasks. This work addresses a critical gap in the emerging field of AI-driven scientific discovery, where LLM agents are increasingly used to control instruments like microscopes and synchrotrons. By highlighting the limitations of current benchmarks, it guides future development of more robust evaluation methods and agent architectures. The framework evaluated one-, two-, and three-agent graph topologies, five LLMs, RAG and context parameters, and operational constraints. Direct comparisons showed clear differences in latency, token use, cost, and failure modes, but surrogate models trained on these results failed to generalize to new tasks, indicating the heterogeneous test suite does not support a task-independent global configuration model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model (LLM) agents are being developed to autonomously control scientific instruments, such as microscopes and synchrotron beamlines, to accelerate research. However, designing such agents involves many choices, including LLM selection, agent topology, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) parameters, and there are few established paradigms. Benchmarks are commonly used to evaluate these agents, but their ability to predict performance on unseen tasks is not well understood. This study provides a systematic framework to evaluate and compare different agent configurations, highlighting the importance of generalization testing.

## [CASCADE: Agentic Framework Predicts Gene Perturbation Effects with Patient Data Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05359) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CASCADE is a new agentic framework that predicts downstream transcriptional effects of gene perturbations using ARACNe regulatory networks exposed via MCP. It validates predictions against real TCGA patient data, showing high concordance for MYC knockdown targets across three cancer types (BRCA: 90.0%, COAD: 72.0%, STAD: 85.7%). This work addresses a gap in validation methods for gene perturbation prediction tools by testing direction of change against real patient data, rather than just membership in known cancer genes. It could improve confidence in computational predictions for cancer research and drug development, though it does not yet outperform existing curated gene-set baselines. CASCADE's validation uses focal-gene copy-number amplification as a dosage-based proxy for the inverse of knockdown. The framework also benchmarks an LLM-based agent's grounding of natural-language requests into MCP tool calls, achieving 71.4% exact match with a local model and 85.7% with a larger model, but both fail on ambiguous queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: ARACNe is an algorithm for reconstructing gene regulatory networks from expression profiles, and TCGA is a comprehensive cancer genomics database. CASCADE uses these precomputed networks to predict perturbation effects, and its validation approach is more rigorous than previous methods that only checked gene membership.

## [DoctorAgents: Agentic AutoML for Small Clinical Temporal Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DoctorAgents is a new agentic AI framework that uses specialized LLM agents for generation, validation, and refinement to iteratively optimize AutoML pipelines for small clinical temporal data. It replaces exhaustive search with reasoning-driven refinement via textual gradient descent, outperforming established AutoML baselines across diverse clinical tasks. This framework addresses a critical gap in AutoML for small, heterogeneous clinical datasets, where traditional brute-force methods are inefficient and lack interpretability. By enabling reasoning-driven pipeline refinement, DoctorAgents could accelerate the development of reliable ML models for high-stakes medical decision-making, benefiting both ML and healthcare communities. DoctorAgents employs three specialized LLM agents for generation, validation, and refinement, and uses textual gradient descent to backpropagate natural-language feedback for targeted updates. The framework produces more interpretable task-specific representations, as demonstrated across diverse clinical tasks in the paper.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: AutoML aims to automate the construction of machine learning pipelines, but traditional systems often rely on brute-force search over predefined spaces, which is inefficient for small, temporal clinical data. Textual gradient descent is a method that optimizes discrete text (e.g., prompts) using LLM-based critique feedback instead of numerical gradients, enabling reasoning-driven refinement. DoctorAgents applies this concept to AutoML, using LLM agents to reason about and improve pipelines iteratively.

## [CANOE: Contestable Argumentative Multi-Agent Framework for Care Plans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05391) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CANOE is a new multi-agent neuro-symbolic framework that combines an Arena-based Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Framework (A-QBAF) with human-in-the-loop contestation for transparent and safe care plan coordination. It was introduced in a preprint on arXiv (2608.05391) and evaluated on Discharge Me! and MedicalRAG datasets. This framework addresses critical limitations of monolithic LLM pipelines in healthcare, where transparency and safety are paramount. By enabling human contestation and argumentation-based reasoning, CANOE could improve trust and adoption of AI-assisted care planning, potentially impacting patient outcomes and clinical workflows. CANOE consists of five modules: complexity assessment, adaptive team recruitment, role-based argumentative computation via A-QBAF, human-in-the-loop contestation, and care-plan synthesis. Evaluation metrics include ROUGE-L, AlignScore, MEDCON F1, FKGL, and LLM-as-a-judge, showing that medically fine-tuned models achieve the strongest clinical correctness and safety.

The paper introduces the Pedagogical Suitability Index (PSI), a composite metric with six sub-scores, to evaluate how well LLM-based AI tutors align with learner readiness and curriculum progression. It also proposes a PSI-guided regeneration protocol that improved 82.3% of weak-performing cases across four tested models. This addresses a critical gap in educational AI by moving beyond mere answer correctness to measure pedagogical appropriateness, which is essential for effective tutoring. The findings suggest that learner- and curriculum-aware alignment matters more than model category, potentially guiding the development of more effective AI tutoring systems. The study evaluated four LLM tutors (ChatGPT, Gemini, Gemma4, and Qwen3) across 240 scenario-based evaluations using paired standard and defective prompts. Baseline PSI scores ranged from 0.557 to 0.638, with no clear separation between open-weight and closed models, and overall PSI remained stable under prompt perturbations (Delta = -0.002).

## [Mixed-Stakeholder Deliberation Reveals Nuanced Views on AI Policing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05418) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A mixed-stakeholder deliberation workshop involving 30 community representatives, police officers, and academics assessed 13 AI use cases in policing, finding that participants rejected only three outright, with recidivism risk assessment being the most contested. The study foregrounded racial bias and found that discussions focused on effectiveness, benefit, and equity rather than narrowing to bias alone. This research addresses the critical issue of racial bias in AI policing by including affected communities in decision-making, a rare practice. The findings could inform policy and governance frameworks for AI adoption in law enforcement, potentially leading to more equitable and trusted AI systems. The workshop rejected recidivism risk assessment due to objections to its premise rather than implementation, echoing concerns like those about COMPAS. The study suggests that foregrounding racial equity broadens deliberation to fundamental questions of effectiveness and benefit, reminiscent of the curb-cut effect in inclusive design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: AI tools are increasingly used in policing, but racial bias is a documented risk, and affected communities are often excluded from adoption decisions. Deliberative stakeholder workshops are a method to bring diverse perspectives together, and recidivism risk assessment tools like COMPAS have faced criticism for potential bias, prompting regulatory attention such as the EU AI Act.

## [SCP-NL2TL: Selective Conformal Prediction for Trustworthy NL-to-TL Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05439) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SCP-NL2TL, a selective translation framework that combines conformal prediction with semantic verification to decide when natural language to temporal logic translations can be trusted. It provides a distribution-free bound on the rate of accepting incorrect specifications and screens out-of-distribution inputs using a conformal anomaly detector. This work addresses a critical reliability issue in safety-critical autonomous systems, where untrusted translations can lead to unsafe behavior. By enabling AI systems to abstain when uncertain, it lays a foundation for trustworthy natural language interfaces in robotics and formal methods. The framework uses two complementary black-box signals: back-translation fidelity and dispersion of repeated translations under exact semantic equivalence, which jointly separate incorrect translations more sharply. Experiments on STL, LTL, and SpaTiaL demonstrate improved reliability, robustness under cross-tier shifts, and effective uncertainty-aware abstention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal logics such as LTL and STL are formal languages used to specify and verify system behavior in robotics and autonomous systems. Translating natural language instructions into these specifications is challenging, and existing models often generate unreliable outputs. Conformal prediction is a distribution-free framework that provides statistical guarantees on prediction sets, and selective prediction allows abstention when confidence is low.

## [Stochasticity Reducible but Sequencing Remains NP-Hard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05455) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that stochastic instructional sequencing can be exactly reduced to a deterministic shortest-path problem on the lattice of prerequisite order ideals, yet the problem remains NP-hard even under strong restrictions. The authors demonstrate hardness via reduction from feedback arc set in tournaments, even with no prerequisite edges and unit costs. This result clarifies the computational complexity of instructional sequencing, showing that stochasticity is not the source of difficulty—combinatorial structure is. It has implications for educational technology and algorithm design, guiding practitioners toward approximation or heuristic methods for large-scale sequencing problems. The paper also shows that when transfer preferences remain jointly acyclic with prerequisites, any topological order is optimal, and fixed prerequisite width yields polynomial-time dynamic programming. A computable diagnostic, mΔ, bounds the value of sequencing; experiments on 70,893 interactions from an introductory CS course certify a 'doubly easy' regime, while constructed instances realize a challenging regime where exact A* with a consistent heuristic expands only linearly many states.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Instructional sequencing is the problem of ordering learning concepts that have prerequisite dependencies. The stochastic shortest path problem is a generalization of the shortest path problem where actions have probabilistic outcomes. This paper studies sequencing as a stochastic shortest path problem where attempting a concept succeeds with a state-dependent probability.

## [StepReflect: Structured Reflection Boosts Mobile GUI Agent Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05587) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StepReflect introduces a supervised structured prediction method for per-step GUI reflection, achieving 82.16% transition-level accuracy on AndroidWorld, surpassing zero-shot GPT-5.2 by 11.83 percentage points. It also improves task success in three of four agent configurations and reduces paid API charges in all four. This work offers a practical, locally deployable alternative to repeated frontier-model reflection for long-horizon mobile GUI agents, potentially reducing costs and improving reliability. It addresses a key bottleneck in autonomous mobile agents by aligning reflection with the structured nature of GUI state transitions. StepReflect is an 8B model trained via a staged pipeline combining supervised fine-tuning, teacher-student distillation, and preference- and reward-based refinement. It uses explicit transition specifications and paired visual evidence as structured inputs, and its online evaluation spans M3A, Agent-SAMA, MAI-UI-8B, and Seed-2.0-Pro.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Mobile GUI agents are AI systems that autonomously interact with mobile apps by observing screens and performing actions, often using large language models (LLMs) for decision-making. Reflection, or evaluating each action's outcome, is crucial for long-horizon tasks but is typically done via costly open-ended multimodal reasoning. Structured prediction is a supervised learning paradigm that outputs structured objects (e.g., sequences or graphs) rather than scalar values, which fits the structured nature of GUI state transitions. Teacher-student distillation is a model compression technique where a smaller student model learns from a larger teacher model's outputs.

## [B-EUR Model Quantifies Value of Design Actions via Uncertainty Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Bayesian Expected Uncertainty Reduction (B-EUR) model, which formalizes the value of a candidate design action as its expected reduction of epistemic uncertainty about action-outcome relations. The model was validated through simulations and human experiments using a graph-shape guessing task. This model provides a computational account of how designers evaluate candidate actions under uncertainty, addressing an open question in the Uncertainty Driven Action (UDA) model. It has implications for decision theory, human-AI interaction, and practical design practices such as constructing prototype sets and organizing feedback. The model examines two environmental properties: generalizability (how far knowledge extends to neighboring candidates) and outcome discriminability (how clearly outcomes can be distinguished). Simulations showed that epistemic value follows an inverted-U relationship with generalizability and increases with outcome discriminability, while human experiments confirmed these patterns in subjective value and choice behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: The B-EUR model builds on the Uncertainty Driven Action (UDA) model, which unifies perspectives on design activity by focusing on uncertainty reduction as a driver of action. In design, individuals often face limited trial budgets and must decide which actions to try to learn about action-outcome relations. The model uses Bayesian inference to quantify epistemic uncertainty and its expected reduction, providing a normative framework for evaluating design options.

## [Refining Over Resampling: Test-Time Self-Correction for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05643) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a verifier-free breadth-depth refinement framework that combines sampling, iterative self-critique and self-correction, and majority voting to improve LLM reasoning at test time. It consistently outperforms greedy decoding, majority voting, verifier-based best-of-N, beam search, and lookahead decoding across multiple math benchmarks and open-weight models. This work addresses the diminishing returns of wider sampling and the calibration issues of verifier-based selection, offering a more effective use of test-time compute. It could lead to more reliable and accurate LLM reasoning without relying on external reward models, benefiting applications in math problem solving and beyond. The method samples multiple independent reasoning rollouts, refines each through iterative self-critique and self-correction, then aggregates via majority voting. For instance, with Qwen2.5-1.5B, accuracy on MATH500 improves from the strongest verifier-based baseline to 58.0%, and on AMC from 25.0% to 32.5%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling improves LLM reasoning by using additional inference compute, but wider sampling alone can suffer from diminishing returns. Verifier-based selection depends on the calibration of an external reward model, which can be unreliable. Self-correction is a prompting pattern where the model reviews its own initial answer, identifies errors, and produces a revised answer. Majority voting aggregates multiple answers to mitigate randomness in LLM responses.

<a id="item-166"></a>
## [INTraj: Unified Trajectory Prediction with Planning and Reaction Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05673) ⭐️ 7.0/10

INTraj is a new unified framework for trajectory prediction that explicitly decomposes social influence into two stages: a planning stage that constructs reference trajectories using future social information, and a reaction stage that recovers local adjustments from the residual. It achieves consistent improvements on four standard benchmarks, including Argoverse 2, Argoverse 2-ped, ETH/UCY, and SDD, with state-of-the-art performance in several settings. This work addresses a clear gap in existing trajectory prediction methods by distinguishing the functional roles of social influence in planning versus reaction, which improves prediction stability and accuracy, particularly in final displacement error (FDE) and long-horizon consistency. The unified framework supports both multi-target and single-target paradigms, making it broadly applicable to autonomous driving and other fields requiring accurate trajectory forecasting. INTraj decomposes social influence into a planning stage that uses future social information to construct reference trajectories and a reaction stage that recovers local adjustments from the residual between full-context prediction and the reference. The framework is validated on four benchmarks and achieves consistent improvements, with code publicly available at https://github.com/11isnotavailable/INTraJ.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Trajectory prediction is crucial for autonomous driving and robotics, where agents must anticipate the future positions of other moving objects. Traditional methods often model social interactions implicitly, but recent work has shifted toward structured formulations that explicitly model social influence. However, most existing methods do not distinguish between the different functional roles that social influence plays during planning and reaction. INTraj addresses this by proposing a two-stage decomposition, which aligns with the observation that agents typically form motion plans by anticipating others' future behaviors before making local reactive adjustments.

## [Physically Grounded Anomaly Labels for Volve Field Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05685) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces physically grounded anomaly labels for the Volve field dataset, validated against engineering documents, and evaluates a baseline and a dual-head model for anomaly detection and classification. The dataset, labels, code, and models are released publicly under CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0. This work addresses a critical gap in real-world anomaly detection, where labeled fault data is scarce, by providing transparent, physically grounded labels that can be used to train and evaluate models. It demonstrates that such labels are learnable and can generalize across wells, which is significant for the oil and gas industry and other domains with similar challenges. The dual-head model simultaneously predicts event presence and event type, while the unsupervised baseline identifies similar regions without seeing labels, indicating label consistency. The supervised model performs well on event presence and type across unseen wells but only roughly localizes events in time.

This paper constructs labels by cross-referencing sensor patterns with engineering documents to ensure physical plausibility.

RA-CAD introduces a state-aware agent that iteratively generates, executes, critiques, and rewrites CAD code, with an explicit critique loop. It achieves state-of-the-art execution validity and geometric quality on CADFusion and Text2CAD benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in feedback utilization for text-to-CAD generation, making critique a learnable policy decision rather than a fixed external input. It could significantly advance AI-assisted design automation, benefiting engineers and designers who rely on parametric CAD tools. RA-CAD uses CAD Code Bootstrapping (CCB) for supervised fine-tuning and Feedback-Driven Agent Optimization (FAO) with trajectory-level Group Relative Policy Optimization, assigning terminal F1 and Chamfer Distance rewards. The agent conditions its critique on the design instruction, current code, and execution feedback, enabling outcome-aligned revisions.

## [When Do Prompt-Side Agent Playbooks Transfer?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05778) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates the transferability of prompt-side agent playbooks under a shared distill-validate-transfer protocol, finding that transfer benefits are inconsistent across benchmarks and can lead to cost inflation and runtime shifts. 这些发现对部署LLM代理的从业者具有重要意义，因为它们警示不要假设剧本可以普遍重用。研究强调需要进行目标侧验证，并表明冻结迁移是一种有条件的冷启动选项，而非默认策略。 On ALFWorld, transfer was beneficial under controlled greedy decoding, but on TAU2-Bench, only one of 135 route-level effects survived Holm correction. On XBench-DeepSearch, a context-runtime shift caused repeated queries, delayed stopping, and substantial cost inflation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt-side playbooks are compact instructions distilled from successful agent trajectories, used to guide tool-using language agents without retraining. The distill-validate-transfer protocol involves a teacher model distilling a playbook, validating it on source data, and then freezing it for transfer to new settings. Holm correction is a multiple testing procedure that controls the family-wise error rate, used here to assess the significance of many route-level comparisons.

It reviews related advances and identifies gaps in agentic maturity evaluation. ## [Cautious Context Steering Improves LM Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05813) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Cautious Context Steering (CCS), a method that adds a lightweight adapter to a frozen backbone language model to dynamically decide at each decoding step whether and how strongly user context should influence generation. A single CCS adapter trained on one dataset improves generation quality both in-domain and across four out-of-distribution personalization benchmarks. CCS addresses data sparsity and generalization issues in existing personalization methods by avoiding per-user fine-tuning and reducing inference cost compared to Context Steering (CoS). This could enable more efficient and scalable personalized AI systems that adapt to new users and domains without extensive retraining. CCS learns the adapter behavior from an oracle context-conditioned LM and preserves the base LM when context is not helpful. It avoids the additional context-conditioned forward pass required by CoS, substantially reducing inference cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Personalizing language models to individual user preferences is important for aligning responses with diverse goals. Existing methods either train separate adapters per user, suffering from data sparsity, or use in-context learning (ICL) and Context Steering (CoS), which do not adapt context influence across decoding steps. CCS introduces a lightweight adapter that dynamically controls context influence, leveraging the base LM's pretrained capabilities without per-user training.

The paper introduces ViSR-KGC, a novel framework that combines representation learning, vision-language models (VLMs), and commonsense knowledge to perform visual subgraph reasoning for multimodal knowledge graph completion (MMKGC). It converts query-aware subgraphs into visually interpretable images and uses a unified prompt with entity images and text to infer missing entities. ViSR-KGC addresses a critical gap in existing MMKGC methods by preserving structural topology and visual information, which are often lost in LLM-based linearization approaches. This could improve performance on knowledge graph completion tasks, especially when relation-specific evidence is limited, and advance multimodal reasoning in AI systems. The framework first learns multimodal embeddings, then extracts a compact, query-aware subgraph from the MMKG. It selects a layout strategy empirically to transform the subgraph into an image, and combines the visualized subgraph, entity images, textual descriptions, and candidate answers into a unified prompt for the VLM.

This paper introduces C-SUITEBENCH, a multimodal benchmark for CEO-level decision-making, and finds that while visual inputs improve evidence-centric reasoning, they degrade constrained resource allocation across all nine tested models. This reveals a multimodal integration paradox, showing that visual perception and constrained action are separable bottlenecks in multimodal agents. The findings have practical implications for designing executive AI systems, suggesting that indiscriminate visual augmentation can harm high-stakes decisions. The benchmark includes five decision tasks under paired text-only and multimodal conditions across 50 scenarios. Ablation experiments show that the failure in resource allocation emerges from signal crowding, where each visual channel helps individually but their combination disrupts constraint satisfaction during decoding.

AppDeltaWorld introduces a transition-grounded delta code world model that predicts the next GUI as a reachable code update, specifically generating Level-2 executable HTML conditioned on current screen, action, and retrieved Level-1 HTML references. The model uses a two-level HTML approach: Level-1 app-specific references retrieved under an action-transition constraint, and Level-2 executable HTML generated with visual assets inserted into image slots before browser rendering. As a training environment, it supports filtered closed-loop SFT data construction, and world-model-based test-time reinforcement learning enables policy adaptation without additional real-app interaction.

ECG-LENS is an end-to-end framework that integrates multi-lead signal modeling, diagnosis-aware representations, and clinically grounded text generation to automatically produce ECG reports. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods on PTB-XL and MIMIC-IV-ECG datasets, with absolute gains of 4.0% in METEOR, 6.3% in ROUGE-L, and 11.5% in F1-ECGBERT. Automating ECG report generation can reduce clinicians' interpretive workload, improve diagnostic efficiency, and expand access to cardiac assessment in underserved communities. This framework addresses a significant gap in automated clinical documentation and has potential for real-world clinical impact. ECG-LENS uses lead-wise encoders to preserve localized waveform morphology and a global encoder to capture inter-lead dependencies. It also introduces an ECG-specific report-preprocessing strategy and a new metric, F1-ECGBERT, which measures agreement between diagnostic labels extracted from generated and reference reports.

This paper introduces a novel Temporal-Enhanced framework with bidirectional temporal alignment for climate data super-resolution, addressing the limitations of single-frame methods and optical flow in noisy climate data. The framework includes Paired Latent Mapping, Bidirectional Temporal Alignment, and Temporal Enhanced Super-resolution, and demonstrates superior performance on large-scale real-world datasets. This work fills a significant gap in climate data super-resolution by leveraging temporal correlations, which can improve the accuracy of high-resolution climate predictions. It is relevant to both AI/ML and climate science communities, potentially enabling more cost-effective generation of fine-grained climate data for meteorological forecasting and decision support. The framework uses Paired Latent Mapping to unify latent spaces for spatial alignment and noise reduction, and Bidirectional Temporal Alignment to capture temporal correlations by training forward and backward networks on consecutive latent frames. Experiments on large-scale real-world datasets show superior performance, though the paper does not provide specific quantitative results in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Climate data super-resolution (SR) aims to generate high-resolution climate data from low-resolution inputs, which is crucial for meteorological predictions but often costly to obtain. Recent deep learning methods have focused on single-frame spatial information, neglecting temporal correlations. Optical flow, a common temporal alignment method, is ineffective for climate data due to its stochastic and noisy nature, motivating the need for a tailored framework.

## [HERALD: Counterfactual Audits and Minimal Repairs for Proof-of-Retrieval Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06012) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HERALD introduces an offline audit framework for proof-of-retrieval rewards, revealing a label-free citation-laundering attack and an inclusion-minimal repair that eliminates the attack with high confidence. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety by providing a method to audit and repair proof-of-retrieval rewards in search agents, which is essential for building trustworthy retrieval-augmented systems. The findings have potential impact on the design of robust reward functions and could influence future research on counterfactual auditing. The audit uses exact same-question interventions, separates candidate-visible from oracle information, and enumerates detector contracts before policy optimization. On four Qwen3-8B pools from HotpotQA, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and MuSiQue, the repair R[L] achieves zero empirical ASR with a 0.50% one-sided cluster upper bound, but fails to meet the EM non-inferiority gate on MuSiQue.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Search-agent rewards typically combine answer quality, citation grounding, tool cost, and anti-hacking terms, but a high score does not necessarily mean the cited evidence was retrieved. Proof-of-retrieval rewards aim to ensure that cited evidence is actually retrieved, but they can be vulnerable to attacks such as citation laundering. HERALD provides a counterfactual audit framework to systematically test and repair such reward functions.

## [Misleading Histories Hijack Tool-Use Agents; New Benchmark and Fix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06057) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a paired benchmark with Original, Polluted, and Oracle State views to evaluate tool-calling agents' robustness to misleading multi-turn histories, and proposes a method called 'ours' that transfers an Oracle-conditioned teacher policy to a student via soft supervision. On Qwen3-1.7B, the method achieves 87.0% Balanced Tool-Use Accuracy, outperforming existing baselines. This work identifies history reliability as a distinct bottleneck in tool-use agents, showing that misleading histories can flip 32.1% of correct decisions. The proposed scalable solution improves robustness and transfers to clean histories and external benchmarks, which is crucial for deploying reliable AI agents in persistent interactions. The benchmark includes eleven gold-preserving interventions that isolate failures in decision state, entity binding, and interface execution. The method scales consistently: an 8B teacher raises a 1.7B student to 91.9%, and an 8B student reaches 93.0%, with transfer to unseen functions and noisy multi-hop QA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-calling agents infer task state from accumulated dialogue and tool traces, but in persistent interactions, historical traces may become outdated yet remain structurally valid. This can mislead the model's policy, causing incorrect tool use. The paper addresses this by providing a benchmark and a training method to improve robustness.

## [Randomized Audit Finds Survey-Country Metadata Can Mislead LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06085) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A randomized audit tested whether disclosing the random origin of survey-country labels reduces their influence on LLM social inference. Results show that disclosure did not attenuate the spurious country-directed shifts, while verified country metadata improved forecast accuracy. This work highlights a subtle but important bias source in LLM-based social inference: even random metadata can spuriously shift predictions, and transparency alone may not mitigate it. It underscores the need for careful metadata handling and bias mitigation in AI systems used for social science research. The primary panel included 72 records, where opaque and disclosed-random labels each produced country-direction shifts of 0.214, with paired attenuation of 0.0003 (95% CI [-0.0157, 0.0166]). Verified country reduced Brier loss by 0.040 (95% CI [0.024, 0.056]), while random-label regret included zero. The dataset PROV-FORECAST contains 14,400 paired item-level probability distributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used for social inference, such as predicting survey responses, and they may rely on metadata like survey country. Brier loss is a common metric for evaluating probabilistic forecasts, measuring the mean squared difference between predicted probabilities and actual outcomes. This audit uses a randomized design to isolate the causal effect of metadata on LLM outputs.

## [ECHO: Local Agentic Health Assistant with Temporal Memory and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06110) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ECHO is a locally-deployable agentic health assistant that integrates a ReAct-based chatbot with 17 clinical tools, a temporal knowledge graph, a two-stage hybrid safety layer, and a multimodal speech assessment module. It achieves a 94.9% tool-execution pass rate, 88.8% safety accuracy, and a mean macro F1 of 0.652 for speech-based emotion, depression, and pain estimation. ECHO addresses critical needs in chronic care by providing a privacy-preserving, on-device AI assistant that can run on consumer hardware, supporting GDPR and KVKK compliance. Its hybrid safety layer and temporal memory are notable contributions that could enhance trust and reliability in healthcare AI applications. The system uses LangGraph for orchestration, a rule-based layer for crisis/jailbreak detection in under 1ms, and a signed GNN with APPNP-style propagation for boundary cases, achieving 90.6% unsafe recall on a Turkish health dataset. The speech module combines Whisper and BERT with cross-attention fusion, and the entire web application runs locally with no external data transmission.

**Background**: Agentic AI systems use large language models to autonomously perform tasks, often with tools and memory. ReAct is a prompting framework that interleaves reasoning and acting, while LangGraph is an open-source orchestration framework for building stateful agents. Temporal knowledge graphs store time-stamped relationships, enabling persistent memory across sessions. Safety in healthcare AI is critical, requiring both fast rule-based checks and nuanced classification of clinical intent.

## [Anacreon: Mixture-of-Minds for Individual-Level Human Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Anacreon, an audience simulation model that uses a mixture-of-minds approach with authorship embeddings and cluster-specific adapters to improve individual-level prediction in LLM-based human simulation. It achieves a state-of-the-art ordinal alignment of 0.775 on a large external survey. This work addresses a known limitation of LLM simulators—flattening heterogeneity—by targeting individual-level accuracy, which is crucial for applications like market research and social science. It demonstrates a practical method to reduce social biases and prompt brittleness, potentially improving the reliability of AI-driven human behavior prediction. Anacreon is built on a Gemma 4 12B base model, learns authorship embeddings to separate individuals, clusters a real qualitative corpus around seed people, and trains a dedicated adapter for each cluster. It also uses chain-of-emotion prompting, shuffles response options to reduce prompt brittleness, and balances the training distribution to reduce positive bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can simulate human responses, but they often capture only central tendencies and miss individual variability. Statistical methods work well for populations but fail at the individual level. Anacreon aims to bridge this gap by using a mixture-of-minds architecture, where different adapters specialize in different subpopulations, and by incorporating psychological and demographic data to better model individual differences.

## [FinEvo-Bench: Longitudinal Benchmark for Self-Evolving Financial Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06144) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FinEvo-Bench introduces a longitudinal benchmark with 120 real-case tasks across 20 business scenes in six financial domains, designed to evaluate self-evolving agents' ability to improve over time. It compares four scaffolds using the Qwen3.7-Max backbone, with Letta achieving the highest evolved score (91.65) and Codex showing the largest self-evolution gain (+19.37). This benchmark addresses a critical gap in agent evaluation by measuring whether experience from prior tasks improves future performance, which is essential for real-world deployment in professional workflows. It provides a standardized way to assess self-evolution capabilities, potentially guiding the development of more adaptive and efficient LLM agents in finance and beyond. The benchmark includes institution-provided professional procedures and manually reviewed rubrics for task quality and financial compliance. It uses three independently shuffled task streams and paired non-evolving controls to estimate self-evolution gains, with an independent Claude Code scoring agent backed by Claude Opus 4.6 evaluating outputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Most existing agent benchmarks evaluate tasks independently, failing to measure whether an agent learns from past experiences. Self-evolving agents aim to improve over time by retaining and applying knowledge from previous tasks, but prior benchmarks have not jointly covered professional workflows, open-ended deliverables, and multi-aspect evaluation. FinEvo-Bench fills this gap by providing a longitudinal setup where tasks are related and ordered, allowing measurement of self-evolution gains.

## [PaDoc: Layout-Grounded Parallel Decoding for Efficient Document Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06146) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PaDoc introduces a layout-grounded parallel decoding method for document parsing, which treats predicted layouts as branching structures over a shared page representation, reducing decoding depth to the longest layout-content path. It achieves this within a single multimodal large language model (MLLM) using packed variable-length ancestor attention and masked parallel decoding. This work addresses a key inefficiency in end-to-end document parsers by enabling region-level parallelism without repeated visual prefills or fragmented page context. It could significantly improve throughput and latency for document AI applications, making it a meaningful contribution to the field of multimodal LLMs and document understanding. On OmniDocBench Full, PaDoc achieves an Overall layout F1 of 91.1 and a top-tier Overall score of 94.24 among end-to-end parsers, with the best Text Edit (0.038) and Formula CDM (95.59). On a 384-page subset with one A800 GPU, it improves valid-page throughput by 67.4-118% and reduces P95 latency by 39.2-54.9% relative to a same-backbone Sequential SFT baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: End-to-end document parsers typically serialize page layouts and regional contents into a single autoregressive sequence, forcing dependencies that increase decoding length with total content. Crop-based two-stage parsers offer region-level parallelism but require repeated visual prefills and fragmented page context. PaDoc's approach factorizes regional content generation under a shared page-image and layout prefix, exposing parallelism without these drawbacks.

## [CogVis: Cognitive Memory Framework for Open-Vocabulary Change Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06150) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CogVis introduces a cognitive memory-guided framework that reformulates open-vocabulary change detection (OVCD) into a perception-memory-verification paradigm, decoupling temporal perception from semantic discrimination. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on seven benchmarks and improves inference throughput by 28.50%. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing OVCD methods that entangle temporal and semantic processing, leading to instability and redundant computation. By decoupling these processes, CogVis improves efficiency and stability, which is vital for large-scale earth-surface monitoring applications. CogVis consists of three components: a Scene Change Perceptron (SCP) that extracts a reusable category-agnostic change prior, a Semantic Memory Calibrator (SMC) that adjusts decision thresholds per image-query, and an Adaptive Region Filter (ARF) that filters candidates based on semantic, temporal, and structural reliability. The framework is evaluated on seven benchmarks covering semantic change detection, binary change localization, and building-damage assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Open-vocabulary change detection (OVCD) is a remote-sensing task that uses arbitrary text prompts to localize and semantically identify changes between two temporal images. Existing methods often entangle temporal perception, semantic discrimination, and region verification, leading to unstable results and redundant computation. CogVis draws inspiration from human visual change perception, which separates the detection of change from the identification of what changed.

This paper introduces a schema-based framework that uses generative AI to extract hierarchical, nested information from unstructured text in a single zero-shot call, and proposes a path-based semantic matching algorithm for automated evaluation against a gold standard. This framework significantly reduces extraction time (about 30x faster than human experts) while achieving high F1 scores (>90% for 12 of 14 attributes) on NICE documents, demonstrating potential to streamline document processing in domains like health technology assessment. The framework uses a schema as an information model encoding domain knowledge, enabling extraction of attributes with variable cardinality. The evaluation rubric classifies matches as exact, semantic, useful, or non-match, using generative AI for semantic comparison. Experiments used Claude Opus 3 and demonstrated generalizability across different generative AI models and transferability across HTA organizations and languages.

## [Hybrid Ranker Beats Knowledge Graph for Agent Skill Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06196) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint compares a hybrid ranker (lexical + dense embeddings) against a typed knowledge graph for retrieving skills from a 690-skill library, finding the ranker achieves 73.5% hit@5 while the graph is significantly worse (-11.2 points, p=0.0007). This matters because AI agents increasingly rely on large skill libraries, and efficient retrieval is critical for autonomous sequencing. The findings challenge the assumption that adding structural knowledge graphs improves retrieval, guiding future agent design toward simpler, ranker-based approaches. The graph's LLM-generated edges add no benefit over free embedding neighbors, and 73% of ranker-missed queries are unreachable via the graph due to a pre-filter topology bound. Additionally, evaluating on author-written queries overstates hit@5 by up to 44 points, which would have hidden these results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs organize data as entities and relationships, often used to encode workflow dependencies like prerequisites and data flow. In AI agent systems, skill retrieval decides which skills to load into context; hybrid rankers combine lexical and semantic search, while knowledge graphs aim to provide structured navigation.

## [TS-RAG: Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06223) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TS-RAG introduces a novel retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) framework specifically designed for time series forecasting, using specially designed reference tokens to fuse input sequences with retrieved similar sequences. The framework achieves state-of-the-art performance on several real-world forecasting benchmarks. This work extends the successful RAG paradigm from large language models to time series forecasting, addressing a gap in the field. It could significantly improve forecasting accuracy and robustness, benefiting applications in finance, energy, and other domains that rely on accurate time series predictions. The framework introduces specially designed reference tokens to effectively fuse information from the input sequence with retrieved similar sequences, enabling a more robust capture of complex temporal dynamics. It is designed to overcome limitations of time series models, such as limited training data and smaller parameter scales, which prevent direct application of RAG as in language models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enhances large language models by retrieving and incorporating relevant external information. Time series forecasting involves predicting future values based on historical data, and deep learning models like transformers have shown promise but often suffer from limited data and generalization issues. TS-RAG applies RAG to time series forecasting by retrieving similar sequences as references, aiming to improve accuracy and interpretability.

## [Improving Realism of Synthetic Clinical Benchmarks Under Utility Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06265) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method to improve the realism of synthetic clinical benchmarks while respecting utility constraints, addressing a gap where benchmarks pass utility checks but remain structurally unrealistic. The approach is instantiated on a care-gap benchmark derived from Synthea-generated patients, with revisions that improve realism without breaking downstream utility. This work is significant for healthcare AI evaluation, especially in privacy-sensitive settings where operational data are scarce. It provides a framework to explicitly optimize benchmark realism, treating utility as a constraint rather than sufficient evidence of quality, which could lead to more reliable evaluation of enterprise AI agents. The baseline benchmark shows extreme thinness: sampled-pair missingness is 79.44%, only 12.75% of rows are actionable, 38.94% of patients have zero actionable measures, and top-three token concentration reaches 100.0%. Two deterministic revisions improve these panels while staying above the utility floor, whereas a naive densification control preserves unrealistic templating.

The framework uses an agentic LLM for clinically informed feature discovery and a compact quantum recurrent network for temporal modeling. ## [Bias Analysis of L2 Speaking Assessment Using CAVs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06300) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends Concept Activation Vectors (CAVs) to analyze bias in transformer-based L2 speaking assessment systems, specifically a text-based BERT grader and a multimodal Whisper-based grader. It also investigates whether sparse autoencoders (SAEs) provide cleaner concept directions in these models. This work addresses fairness in high-stakes AI-based language assessment, ensuring scores reflect proficiency rather than speaker attributes like L1 or age. It provides a method to audit bias in modern transformer models, which are increasingly used in educational technology. The study finds that concept recoverability depends on the representation and architecture, not just the concept itself. SAEs make concepts more linearly recoverable but attenuate sensitivity in low-dimensional layers, highlighting the need to distinguish recoverability from influence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Activation Vectors (CAVs) are interpretability tools that represent human-understandable concepts as directions in a model's activation space. They are used to detect whether a model encodes a concept and whether it influences predictions. BERT is a transformer-based language model, and Whisper is a speech recognition model; both are used in automatic speaking assessment systems.

## [Evidence-Linked Multi-Agent Pipeline for Heart-Failure Feature Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06366) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Nimblemind Multi-Agent System (nMAS), an evidence-linked, rubric-grounded pipeline for automated heart-failure feature engineering from EHR data. Evaluated on 500 dummy patient records, nMAS generated 132 structured and 70 rubric-scored aggregated features, improving held-out AUROC for HFrEF and HFpEF phenotyping. This work addresses a major bottleneck in clinical AI—EHR feature engineering, which consumes 39-45% of data scientists' time. By combining rule-based and LLM-based methods with evidence traceability, nMAS could significantly accelerate clinical research and improve the reliability of AI models for heart failure. nMAS generated features verified for structural integrity, rubric compliance, and provenance, and audited by a restricted LLM. Adding these features improved held-out AUROC from 0.895 to 0.963 for HFrEF and 0.870 to 0.910 for HFpEF, with an independent LLM-based rubric assessment scoring 81.5% of maximum points. However, evaluation was limited to a single-institution cohort, and external validation is needed.

Multi-agent systems (MAS) distribute tasks among specialized agents to improve performance, which nMAS leverages.
