## [Layer-Wise Conductance Framework Reveals Positional Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04098) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a layer-wise conductance framework within a sliding-window design to measure positional bias in short-context language models, revealing that recency bias increases monotonically across layers while primacy bias is subtle and diminishes. This work provides a novel method to quantify how input positions influence predictions at each layer, offering deeper insights into transformer internals. It could inform future model design and interpretability research, particularly for understanding and mitigating positional biases in LLMs. The framework is applied to short-context next-word prediction, and the resulting positional importance profiles are stable across diverse texts and lexical scrambling. The study also finds that positional bias varies by word type: function words exhibit higher recency bias, while content words show higher primacy bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Positional bias in transformers refers to the tendency of models to systematically prefer tokens at specific input positions, regardless of semantic relevance. Prior work has characterized this bias through performance drops in long-context tasks or attention-based analyses, but this paper introduces a layer-wise conductance framework to measure how input positions drive predictions layer by layer.

## [FI-TW: Open Train-Weather Dataset for Railway Delay Analysis in Finland](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16592) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FI-TW, the first publicly available dataset combining Finnish railway operations with synchronized weather data from 2018 to 2024. It integrates operational metrics from Finland's Digitraffic Railway Traffic Service with weather measurements from 209 stations, covering about 38.5 million observations across the 5,915-kilometer rail network. This dataset fills a critical gap in railway delay analysis by integrating weather data, which is particularly relevant for Nordic regions where weather significantly impacts reliability. It enables researchers to develop machine learning models for delay prediction, weather impact assessment, and infrastructure vulnerability mapping, potentially improving railway operations and planning. The dataset includes 28 engineered features, with preprocessing involving spatial fallback algorithms for missing data, cyclical encoding of temporal features, and robust scaling of weather data. A baseline XGBoost regression achieved a Mean Absolute Error of 2.73 minutes for predicting station-specific delays, and winter months show delay rates exceeding 25%.

Spatial fallback algorithms help handle missing data by using data from nearby stations when a specific station's data is unavailable.

This paper proposes a novel trust-based incentive mechanism for semi-decentralized federated learning (FL) that dynamically evaluates participant trust scores based on data quality, model accuracy, consistency, and contribution frequency, and uses these scores to reward honest nodes and penalize malicious ones. It also explores integrating blockchain and smart contracts to automate trust evaluation and incentive distribution, ensuring transparency and decentralization. This work addresses a critical challenge in federated learning—ensuring trust and incentivizing honest participation—which is essential for the practical deployment of decentralized ML systems. By combining trust scores with blockchain, it offers a transparent and fair approach that could reduce risks from malicious participants and foster broader adoption of FL in sensitive domains like healthcare and finance. The proposed framework dynamically computes trust scores based on multiple factors, including data quality, model accuracy, consistency, and contribution frequency. It integrates blockchain and smart contracts to automate trust evaluation and incentive distribution, ensuring transparency and decentralization, though it remains a theoretical framework without empirical validation.

A new preprint compares synthetic survey data from five leading LLMs against a human survey of 420 Silicon Valley coders, finding that while AI outputs are plausible, they fail to capture counterintuitive insights and deviate systematically from real data. This study highlights the limitations of using LLM-generated synthetic data as a substitute for human survey responses, which is increasingly common in research. It underscores the need for validation protocols and reporting standards to ensure responsible use of synthetic data in organizational research. The five models tested were ChatGPT Thinking 5 Pro, Claude Sonnet 4.5 Pro plus Claude CoWork 1.123, Gemini Advanced 2.5 Pro, Incredible 1.0, and DeepSeek 3.2. The study found that all models' outputs grouped together, leaving the real data as an outlier, and none captured the counterintuitive insights that made the human survey valuable.

## [MM-ISTS: Multimodal Vision-Text LLMs for Irregular Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MM-ISTS introduces a novel framework that integrates vision-text large language models (LLMs) with temporal encoding to forecast irregularly sampled time series (ISTS). It proposes a two-stage encoding mechanism, including a Cross-Modal Vision-Text Encoding module and an Adaptive Query-Based Feature Extractor, to enhance contextual understanding and reduce computational costs. This work addresses a significant challenge in real-world time series forecasting, where data are often irregularly sampled and missing. By leveraging multimodal LLMs, it could improve prediction accuracy in domains like healthcare, finance, and IoT, potentially setting a new trend for integrating LLMs with time series analysis. The framework includes a Temporal-Variable Encoder for multi-view embedding fusion and a Multimodal Alignment module with Modality-Aware Gating to bridge modality gaps. The Adaptive Query-Based Feature Extractor compresses MLLM token embeddings to preserve useful knowledge while reducing computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Irregularly sampled time series (ISTS) are common in real-world applications where observations occur at uneven intervals and may have missing values. Traditional forecasting methods often rely on regularly sampled data and struggle to capture contextual semantics. Multimodal LLMs, such as GPT-4V and Gemini, can process both text and images, enabling richer representations. This paper combines these capabilities with temporal encoding to improve ISTS forecasting.

It is compatible with standard confocal microscopes, allowing immediate adoption. The paper introduces Look Twice (LoT), a training-free inference-time framework that leverages the model's internal attention to highlight relevant visual and textual evidence for knowledge-based visual question answering (KB-VQA). LoT improves accuracy by up to +12.5 points across four benchmarks and ten MLLMs without any parameter updates. This work addresses a critical limitation in KB-VQA—noisy retrieval and distracting visual regions—by providing a general, training-free solution that works across multiple MLLMs. It offers a practical way to improve existing models without retraining, which could accelerate deployment and reduce computational costs in real-world applications. LoT filters attention sinks and distracting content, then reformulates the input to explicitly highlight selected evidence before answer generation. It requires no parameter updates, auxiliary models, or architectural modifications, and it also provides additional gains when combined with existing context-refinement strategies.

This paper introduces a stage-wise preference optimization framework that constructs hallucination-focused preference pairs near known failure boundaries, such as ambiguous spatial orientation and OCR uncertainty, and applies Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to reduce hallucination in vision-language models (VLMs). The framework progressively constructs preference pairs focusing on ambiguous spatial orientation, object relationships, OCR uncertainty, and adversarial false-premise training. Hallucinated negatives are generated via minimal perturbations that are visually inconsistent, enabling DPO to better separate grounded reasoning from plausible hallucination.

This framework addresses a gap in federated learning definitions, which have not kept pace with the evolution beyond weights and gradients to include payloads like synthetic data and federated analytics. It provides a structured path for optimizing federated systems for varying hardware and security requirements, benefiting researchers and practitioners in the field. The taxonomy evaluates the three categories based on computational demands, communication costs, and privacy risks. The review highlights a significant shift since 2021 toward diverse messaging paradigms, moving away from standard deep learning updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning is a machine learning technique where multiple entities collaboratively train a model while keeping data decentralized. Traditionally, messages exchanged were model weights and gradients, but modern applications include synthetic data and federated analytics, which involve analyzing data without moving it from its source. This taxonomy helps clarify the trade-offs among different message types.

This integration addresses a critical limitation of LLM-based planners—natural language ambiguity—by ensuring mission plans meet formal specifications, which is essential for safe deployment in real-world robotics. The paper introduces multiple feedback loops in the planning architecture that leverage LTL to verify mission plans against user specifications. Extensive experiments highlight the strengths and limitations of integrating mission verification into a fully autonomous pipeline, particularly regarding an LLM's ability to generate valuable LTL formulas.

## [AgentMeter: A Fair Quality-Efficiency Benchmark for CLI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21140) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentMeter introduces a new benchmark and the AgentMeter Score (AMS) metric that jointly evaluates task quality, budget sensitivity, and zero-reward execution for command-line agents. It also proposes AgentMeter-Opt, a trajectory-grounded optimization framework to create fairer evaluation sets across LM-CLI pairs. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing agent evaluations, which often focus solely on success rate while ignoring cost and efficiency. By enabling better selection of LM-CLI pairs, AgentMeter can help practitioners deploy more cost-effective and reliable command-line agents in real-world applications. AgentMeter provides Benchmark90 as the full validation set and Core30 as a lower-cost subset, covering 24 complete model-CLI configurations. Experiments show that no CLI is universally optimal across language models, and task success, execution cost, and AMS identify different competitive configurations.

## [InferQ: A Database-Oriented Benchmark for Quantum Circuit Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InferQ is a new benchmark that generates SQL workloads for quantum circuit simulation, releasing a dataset of 202,975 circuits. It provides a web-based viewer for searching and downloading circuits and feature records. This benchmark bridges quantum computing and database systems, enabling systematic research in query optimization and physical design for SQL-based quantum simulation. It could lead to more efficient simulation methods and broader adoption of RDBMSs in quantum computing. InferQ generates compositional circuits from templates and extracts static, graph, SQL, and dynamic features. Experiments show RDBMSs achieve better peak memory usage than Qiskit Aer on over 50% of circuits, and lightweight ML models can predict SQL execution preference with up to 95.6% accuracy for runtime and 97.4% for memory.

## [Activation Steering Workflow Improves Role-Conditioned Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00023) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an activation-steering screening workflow for role-conditioned language model agents, applied to OLMo-3-7B-Instruct with a 275-role inventory. The method achieves higher judged role-profile alignment (63.2 vs 41.1) and preserves lexical diversity compared to prior persona-vector methods. This work addresses a critical need in social simulations: ensuring role-conditioned behavior is reliable before deployment. It offers a practical, per-role coefficient selection method that can improve the fidelity of multi-agent simulations, impacting fields like social science research and game development. The workflow involves defining a role profile, extracting a role-specific direction, sweeping four steering coefficients, and evaluating alignment with GPT-4.1-mini judges. Notably, 38 roles declined across all six measured dimensions, highlighting the need for per-role coefficient tuning rather than a uniform high-strength setting.

**Background**: Activation steering is a technique that modifies a language model's hidden states at inference time to shift its behavior. This paper builds on prior work on persona vectors and activation engineering to improve role-conditioned generation.

It employs a multi-agent architecture with a feedback mechanism and a retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipeline, successfully completing all 48 tasks in a benchmark and outperforming other LLM-based approaches, including solving 21 more tasks than GPT-5 with a general RAG pipeline. This addresses the productivity gap in PIC design, where script-based methods require additional API and programming proficiency. By automating script generation, PICopilot can significantly reduce design time and effort, making PIC design more accessible and scalable, which is crucial as PICs grow in complexity for next-generation computing and communication. The framework leverages a multi-agent architecture with a feedback mechanism and a specifically designed RAG pipeline. Experimental results show it achieves a high success rate and reliability without substantial extra latency or cost, outperforming advanced models like GPT-5 on a benchmark of diverse PIC scripting tasks.

## [DisMix: Order-Aware Mixup for Ordinal Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04652) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DisMix introduces an order-aware mixup framework that disentangles ordinal and non-ordinal features using a dual-codebook VQ-VAE, allowing independent mixing of each subspace. It demonstrates superior aggregate performance across four medical imaging datasets compared to six mixup baselines paired with six ordinal classifiers. This work addresses a critical limitation of standard mixup in ordinal classification tasks, which is common in medical disease grading. By preserving ordinal structure during augmentation, DisMix could improve the reliability and accuracy of automated medical image grading systems, potentially aiding clinical decision-making. DisMix uses a dual-codebook VQ-VAE to separate ordinal codes (interpolated for intermediate ranks) and non-ordinal codes (varied for appearance diversity). It remains effective under data scarcity and clinical grading variability, as shown in the experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Ordinal classification is a type of classification where labels have a natural order, such as disease severity grades. Standard mixup augmentation blends images and labels linearly, which can distort the ordinal structure. DisMix leverages a VQ-VAE to disentangle features, enabling order-aware augmentation.

## [InsightEmb: Learning Action-Intent Embeddings for Agentic Insight Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04761) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InsightEmb introduces a contrastive embedding framework that learns progress-oriented retrieval geometry using only mathematical reasoning data, without environment-specific training. It improves performance on dynamic agent tasks and a static skill-retrieval benchmark, surpassing existing reasoning embedding models. This work addresses a critical gap in agentic retrieval by focusing on whether a retrieved insight resolves the agent's current decision bottleneck, rather than just semantic similarity. It enables more effective self-improving agents and suggests that progress-oriented geometry can transfer across domains, reducing the need for expensive environment-specific supervision. InsightEmb jointly learns to align concrete situations with abstract heuristic rules and to cluster reasoning trajectories with similar progress structures. Evaluations were conducted on dynamic agent tasks and a static skill-retrieval benchmark, showing improvements without any environment-specific training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving agents accumulate reusable insights from past trajectories, and retrieval is crucial for turning this experience into actionable guidance. Traditional retrieval methods mainly model semantic similarity, overlooking whether an insight actually helps the agent progress. InsightEmb uses contrastive learning, a technique that trains embeddings by pulling similar pairs together and pushing dissimilar pairs apart, to learn a retrieval geometry that emphasizes progress. This approach is trained on mathematical reasoning data, which is publicly available and avoids the need for expensive environment-specific supervision.

## [A-SR: Self-Evolving Agentic LLMs for Symbolic Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A-SR is a new self-evolving agentic framework for symbolic regression that coordinates formula discovery via role-conditioned evidence views and hierarchical protocols, improving search efficiency and enabling distillation into open-source models. It achieves significant accuracy improvements over baselines on the LLM-SRBench benchmark. This work addresses limitations of existing LLM-guided symbolic regression methods by shifting control from expression edits to role-conditioned evidence views, potentially improving scientific discovery efficiency. It also demonstrates that agentic frameworks can be distilled into smaller open-source models, making advanced AI techniques more accessible. A-SR uses routing among coordination protocols, an online evaluator-reward role policy, and state-routed process memory. It self-evolves at two timescales: within a run (adapting search process without updating LLM parameters) and across runs (distilling trajectories into open-source LLMs). On LSR-Synth domains, A-SR improves Acc@0.01 from 25.79% to 48.30% with Llama3.1-8B, and A-SR-LoRA improves Qwen3-4B from 24.58% to 38.29%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic regression (SR) is a type of regression analysis that searches the space of mathematical expressions to find the model that best fits a dataset, balancing accuracy and simplicity. Traditional methods often use genetic programming, but recent approaches leverage large language models (LLMs) to guide the search. However, existing LLM-guided methods often compress heterogeneous search failures into a scalar score and a single prompt, limiting their effectiveness.

## [OPD-V: Visual On-Policy Self-Distillation with Modality Balance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OPD-V, a visual on-policy self-distillation method that uses modality balance as privileged information to improve visual reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It constructs a Positive Teacher with Zoom-In images and a Negative Teacher with Mask images to define a Modality-Balance Trust Region for selecting on-policy tokens during self-distillation. This work addresses the overlooked issue of modality imbalance in MLLM reasoning, where textual information often dominates and limits the use of privileged information. By leveraging modality balance as a signal, OPD-V consistently improves reasoning performance across multiple benchmarks and backbones while reducing training cost, offering a new direction for post-training of multimodal models. OPD-V employs a dual-teacher architecture: a Positive Teacher with Zoom-In images and a Negative Teacher with Mask images, which exhibit different degrees of modality imbalance. The Modality-Balance Logits Margin between these teachers defines a trust region for selecting on-policy tokens, and experiments cover 6 benchmarks, 4 MLLM backbones, and 5 post-training methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) is a post-training approach where a model serves as both student and teacher, using privileged information from diverse inputs to improve reasoning. Modality imbalance refers to the tendency of multimodal LLMs to over-rely on text while underutilizing visual information, which can hinder effective integration of multimodal inputs. OPD-V builds on these concepts by treating modality balance itself as privileged information.

## [Adversarially Robust Posture Optimization for Military Asset Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05256) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a scenario-weighted adversarially robust optimization engine for the Posture and Sustainment Allocation (PSA) problem, modeled as a finite-horizon Markov Decision Process. It presents two optimizers, CEV and RobustCEV, which outperform greedy heuristics in experiments with 20 assets and 5 theater locations. This work addresses a formally unsolved problem in military operational planning, offering a principled alternative to greedy heuristics that are vulnerable to adversarial targeting. The approach could improve readiness and efficiency in real-world basing decisions, and the methodology may inspire robust optimization techniques in other domains. The CEV optimizer maximizes scenario-weighted expected posture efficiency, while RobustCEV iterates against a Bayesian adversary that updates its targeting distribution. Experiments show the greedy baseline suffers a 25.1% efficiency penalty and a 57.3% readiness collapse, while CEV recovers up to 19.8% efficiency and RobustCEV up to 158% relative to a naive optimizer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Pre-commitment posture refers to assigning military assets to theater locations before conflict scenarios resolve, a critical but unsolved problem in joint operational planning. Current practice uses greedy heuristics that ignore geographic coverage and are vulnerable to adversaries targeting high-value locations. The paper models this as a Markov Decision Process and introduces adversarial robustness to handle adaptive threats.

This work addresses a practical challenge in student mobility programs like Erasmus+, where ensuring course equivalence is time-consuming for administrators. Automating this process could save significant effort and improve consistency in curriculum alignment across universities, benefiting both students and administrative staff. The method extracts course titles, descriptions, and learning outcomes from course webpages, represents them semantically using a BERT-based language model, and computes pairwise similarities. A Random Forest classifier then determines overlap, and the evaluation used data from two universities, including six approved international programs with administrator decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: BERT (Bidirectional Encoder Representations from Transformers) is a pre-trained language model that captures contextual meaning of text, widely used for semantic similarity tasks. This paper investigates the use of Vision Language Models (VLMs) for annotating video game frame sequences with reward signals, revealing that VLMs often struggle with basic questions on racing games and other genres. It also explores countermeasures such as output mixing and prompt optimization, and analyzes how input sequence length, resolution, and question batching affect annotation quality and token consumption. This work highlights the limitations of VLMs in synthetic, variable environments like video games, which is crucial for applications such as conditioned training and offline reinforcement learning. By identifying optimization strategies, it provides practical guidance for leveraging VLMs in game data annotation, potentially accelerating AI development in gaming and related fields. The study focuses on racing video games but notes similar behavior across other genres. Key factors affecting annotation quality include input sequence length, resolution, and question batching, which also impact token consumption. The proposed countermeasures include VLM output mixing and prompt optimization.

## [Hybrid ML Framework for Cattle Weight Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06001) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study introduces a hybrid machine learning framework for herd-level cattle weight forecasting, integrating automated sensing data from 2022-2024 in southeastern Australia. The cascade architecture (GB to RF to NN) achieved the best performance with a test R^2 of 0.889, RMSE of 21.319 kg, and MAE of 15.462 kg. This framework addresses the challenge of irregular livestock observations in commercial grazing systems, offering robust predictions that can support feed allocation, grazing management, and marketing decisions. It demonstrates the potential of hybrid models over traditional recurrent networks in sparse data conditions, contributing to precision agriculture. The framework uses temporal aggregation of animal-level predictions to generate herd-level trajectories, and evaluates four hybrid architectures: residual, stacked, cascade, and ensemble-assisted. Feature importance analysis identified animal age, rainfall, and temperature as dominant predictors, and forecasting error increased with longer horizons.

This paper proposes a dual-level relational framework that combines implicit inter-patch modeling via a convolutional masked autoencoder with explicit graph-based message passing for skin lesion diagnosis. On ISIC-2018, the integrated model achieves 79.27% balanced accuracy, up from 76.17% baseline, and on ISIC-2019 it reaches 60.67%. This work demonstrates that combining implicit and explicit relational biases can improve medical image classification, offering a practical approach for tasks where structural dependencies matter. It could influence future designs of graph-based models in medical imaging and other domains. The framework uses EfficientNetB3 as baseline, a convolutional masked autoencoder for implicit patch relationships, and various graph topologies (grid, random, k-NN) for explicit modeling, with a Graph Attention Network performing best. The improvements are modest but consistent across benchmarks.

## [Compliance-First Agentic AI Architecture for Hospitals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06112) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a multi-layered, compliance-first agentic AI architecture for hospitals, incorporating an Agent Orchestration Layer, a Compliance and Policy Layer with policy-as-code, and a Privacy-Preserving Data Fabric. It demonstrates the architecture using a synthetic hospital dataset and an open prototype, showing reduced task turnaround times and manual documentation effort. This matters because 70-80% of healthcare AI pilots fail to scale due to governance gaps and fragmented integration. The proposed architecture offers a pragmatic blueprint for hospital leaders to move from siloed tools to a governed, compliant, and ROI-focused AI platform, potentially accelerating safe AI adoption in healthcare. The architecture extends existing hospital AI platform models with three layers: Agent Orchestration for multi-agent workflows, Compliance and Policy Layer centralizing policy-as-code for HIPAA, GDPR, EU AI Act, DISHA, DPDP Act, and ISO/IEC standards, and a Privacy-Preserving Data Fabric integrating federated learning, differential privacy, and secure enclaves into HIMS flows. The prototype uses a synthetic but structurally realistic hospital dataset, and the study reports substantial simulated reductions in task turnaround times and manual documentation effort.

## [Generative AI as Epistemic Arbiter Reshapes Collaborative Problem-Solving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05171) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A six-week quasi-experimental study with 201 fifth graders found that generative AI support significantly altered collaborative problem-solving behaviors, increasing social interactions but reducing cognitive ones. The study introduces the concept of GAI as an 'epistemic arbiter' that helps students resolve disagreements constructively. This study provides empirical evidence on how generative AI affects collaborative learning, highlighting a trade-off between social and cognitive engagement. It suggests that AI tools can mediate conflict resolution but may also reduce students' independent reasoning, which has implications for designing AI-integrated educational environments. The study used Chi-square analysis to show significant differences in CPS behavior distributions, and lag sequential analysis revealed distinct interaction patterns: the GAI group showed a robust pathway from task planning to conflict management to solution reasoning, while the control group followed a conventional listening-to-planning transition. Thematic analysis of AI interaction logs indicated that students used AI-generated facts and visualizations to resolve disagreements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 7, 04:00

**Background**: Collaborative problem-solving (CPS) is a key skill in education, involving social and cognitive processes. Generative AI (GAI) tools can provide information and visualizations, potentially acting as an 'epistemic arbiter' that helps students settle disputes. Quasi-experimental studies, unlike randomized controlled trials, lack random assignment but can still estimate causal impacts under certain assumptions. Lag sequential analysis is a statistical method used to identify patterns in sequences of behaviors.

This dissertation introduces a new dataset combining Wikidata and Twitter, and evaluates multiple machine learning models (RF, KNN, LR, SVM, BiLSTM, CNN, BERT) for automatically detecting deaths of social media users from pre- and post-mortem posts. It also offers comparative insights into traditional versus deep learning models for text classification tasks. The study found that TF-IDF consistently outperformed pre-trained word embeddings for traditional models, while Word2Vec was best for deep learning models. Post-mortem tweets showed higher negative sentiment and more frequent use of words related to family, religion, death, and swearing, whereas pre-mortem tweets had more neutral sentiment and informal language.

## [Dynamic Codebook and MLLM Boost Text Steganography Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20269) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a black-box text steganography method that uses a dynamic codebook and a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to improve security and practicality. It replaces fixed codebooks with a dynamic one generated from shared session configurations and the MLLM, and adds a feedback optimization mechanism based on reject sampling. This work addresses key limitations of existing text steganography methods, such as exposure risks in white-box approaches and inflexibility in black-box approaches. It could enable more secure and practical covert communication in online social networks, with potential applications in privacy protection and secure messaging. The method constructs a dynamic codebook via shared session configuration and an MLLM, then uses an encrypted steganographic mapping to embed secret messages during text generation. A feedback optimization mechanism based on reject sampling ensures accurate extraction, and experiments show it outperforms white-box methods in embedding capacity and text quality, while achieving better practicality and flexibility than existing black-box paradigms.

**Background**: Text steganography hides secret messages in cover text. Traditional methods often rely on fixed codebooks or shared language models, which can be insecure or impractical. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can process and generate text along with other modalities, enabling more flexible and dynamic codebook generation. This paper leverages MLLMs to create a dynamic codebook that changes per session, enhancing security.
